Theorems 3.1/3.2, Lemmas 3.5/3.6. The exact PDF and extracted text were read.
Its HTML uses different theorem numbers. Matlas returned unrelated results;
no Matlas grounding is claimed. The identities needed here are derived below.

## 1. Notation, normalization, and deterministic step words

Normalize smoothness to one and the minimizer to zero. Thus

    x_i = x_0 - sum_{t<i} h_t g_t,
    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    Q_i* = f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2.

An actual trajectory has every Q_ij <= 0. A full upper certificate at level q
is the identity

    q||x_0||^2 - 2f_n + sum lambda_ij Q_ij = R,

with all lambda_ij >= 0 and R PSD in the ORIGINAL vectors
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n). An upper bound derived this way uses the true initial
distance; translating/scaling back gives the project's normalization
2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2).

Write P_n = C_n = 1+S_circle(n), D_n = B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n). These aliases
are used only in this note. Their exact recurrences are

    P_0=D_0=1,
    P_n=max_{0<=k<n} Omega(P_k,P_{n-k-1}),
    D_n=max_{0<=k<n} G(P_k,D_{n-k-1}),
    Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

Let k_P(n), respectively k_D(n), be the SMALLEST index attaining the
corresponding maximum. These are exact real comparisons among a finite list
of nested radicals, with a specified tie rule, independent of the objective.
Set p^(0)=d^(0)=(). Recursively define the complete words

    p^(n) = (p^(k), phi(P_k-1,P_l-1), p^(l)),
        k=k_P(n), l=n-k-1,
    d^(n) = (p^(k), psi(P_k-1,(D_l-1)/2), d^(l)),
        k=k_D(n), l=n-k-1.

Here phi and psi are exactly those in accepted b98bb807d514e80d. Induction
gives length n, sums P_n-1 and (D_n-1)/2, respectively. All entries exceed
one: at a primitive join b-1 is the positive root of
r^2+(A+C)r-AC=0; at a dominant join b=1+A/r with r>1 below.
Thus these are actual predetermined legal words at EVERY horizon, with no
assumption on maximizing split patterns. Sections 2--4 specify every one of
their certificate coefficients, not just their scalar values.

## 2. Primitive finite-array identity

For a segment from sample a to sample z, with steps h_a,...,h_(z-1) and sum s,
define

    E_P = (||x_a||^2-||x_z||^2)/2
          + sum_{a<=i<z} h_i Q_i* - s f_z
          - s(s+1)||g_z||^2/2.

We construct a nonnegative finite-node array L_P with zero diagonal such that

    E_P = -sum_{a<=i,j<=z; i!=j} L_P(i,j) Q_ij.          (P)

The empty segment has L_P=0 and E_P=0. All arrays below are embedded at their
actual chronological indices and added if supports overlap.

For a join, let p be the last left sample, p+1 the first right sample, and z
the final sample. Let the left/right step sums be x,y, and b=phi(x,y). Put

    t=(x+b)/(y+2-b)>0.

The denominator is positive: substituting the defining radical gives
1<b<y+2. Let L_L,L_R be the child arrays. The complete recursion is

    L_P = L_L + (1+2t)L_R
          + t sum_{p<i<z} h_i (E_(i,p)+E_(i,z))
          + t(b-1)E_(p,z) + t E_(z,p),                 (P-array)

where E_(i,j) denotes one unit at the indicated ordered pair. Empty sums are
zero. Every displayed coefficient is nonnegative.

Here is the polynomial identity behind the recursion. Put C_R=y(y+1) and
F_j=sum_{p<i<z} h_i Q_(i,j), for j=p,z. Direct substitution of the definition
of Q gives, for ANY bridge b,

    (y+2-b)(f_p-f_z)
      -(y+3b-2b^2)||g_p||^2/2
      -(2y^2+3y+b)||g_z||^2/2
    = 2 E_R - F_p-F_z-(b-1)Q_(p,z)-Q_(z,p).          (P-decrease)

To check it without a black-box inequality, substitute twice the identity

    sum h_i Q_i* = F_j+y(f_j-||g_j||^2/2)
                  + <x_(p+1)-x_z,g_j-x_j>

into 2E_R, then use x_(p+1)=x_p-bg_p. Adding the two last Q terms cancels
the mixed product involving x_(p+1)-x_z and leaves exactly the displayed
two squared-gradient coefficients.

With s=x+b+y and C_L=x(x+1), C=s(s+1), expansion also gives

    E_P = E_L+E_R+(x+b)(f_p-f_z)
          +(C_L+b-b^2)||g_p||^2/2+(C_R-C)||g_z||^2/2.

The defining quadratic b^2+(x+y)b-(xy+2x+2y+2)=0 yields

    (x+b)(y+3b-2b^2)/(y+2-b)=b^2-b-C_L,
    (x+b)(2y^2+3y+b)/(y+2-b)=C-C_R.

Inserting (P-decrease) proves (P-array) and (P) by induction. This proves
the primitive certificate on every formal vector, not merely on a compressed
endpoint matrix.

## 3. Dominant finite-array identity

For a segment with sum y, let its mass be D=2y+1. We construct positive
weights v_i on ALL its samples, summing to D, and a nonnegative finite array
L_D such that

    E_D := ||x_a||^2/2 + sum v_i Q_i* - D f_z
           - ||x_a-sum v_i g_i||^2/2
         = -sum L_D(i,j) Q_ij.                        (D)

For the empty segment take v_a=1 and L_D=0. The identity then follows by
expanding Q_a*.

Join a primitive left segment of sum x to a dominant right segment of mass
D=2y+1, with its weights v and array L_R. Set A=x+1 and

    mu=2A/D,
    r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,
    lambda=r^2=r+mu,
    gamma=A+1=x+2,
    b=1+A/r=psi(x,y).

The new weights u and finite array are

    u_i=h_i                  (i<p),
    u_p=gamma,
    u_i=r v_i                (p<i<=z),
    L_D=L_L+lambda L_R+mu sum_{p<i<=z} v_i E_(i,p).   (D-array)

They have the exact mass

    M=sum u_i=x+gamma+rD=2A+rD=Dr^2
      =G(A,D)=2(x+b+y)+1.

For completeness, the whole algebra follows from the more general identity
in section 6. Setting these parameters makes all three entries of its mixed
matrix K zero: gamma=A+1 gives K_11=0, lambda=r^2 gives K_22=0, and
lambda b=mu+gamma r gives K_12=0. The value-flow equality is mu D=x+gamma.
Thus (D) follows from (P), the right child identity, and the displayed fan.
There is no discarded nonnegative square or omitted mixed term.

## 4. The actual full certificate at exactly 1/B_n

Apply section 3 recursively to the deterministic word d^(n). Its mass is
M=B_n. On I_n={*,0,...,n}, set

    lambda_(i,*)=2u_i/B_n,
    lambda_(*,i)=0,
    lambda_(i,j)=2L_D(i,j)/B_n  (finite i!=j).

Then the exact full identity is

    ||x_0||^2/B_n - 2f_n + sum lambda_ij Q_ij
       = ||x_0-sum u_i g_i||^2/B_n.                  (Full)

Every coefficient and every formal-vector term has now been specified.
The function-value flow is out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), by equating the independent
f_i coefficients in (D). The remainder is a full rank-one PSD quadratic.
Its kernel is x_0=sum u_i g_i. It is not a PSD assertion on a quotient.

The actual source array has no incoming terminal edge. This follows
inductively from (D-array): its left subtree is strictly before the final
sample, and every new edge involving that sample leaves it. Also
u_n=sqrt(B_n), by the dominant recursion u_n=r v_n and B_n=r^2 D.
Consequently, in the exact terminal framework of accepted1252b8823bb5a290,

    alpha_star=1/sqrt(B_n), alpha_j=L_D(n,j)/B_n (j<n),
    V_alpha=(x_0-sum_{i<n}u_i g_i)/sqrt(B_n),
    R_alpha=0 IDENTICALLY on the entire original prefix space.       (Terminal)

The probabilities sum to one by the terminal value flow. Indeed (Full) is
exactly ||g_n-V_alpha||^2, and the terminal identity of that accepted fact
then gives the displayed zero prefix remainder. This records the full
baseline kernel and its mixed-block normalization explicitly; the claim is
not just that R_alpha vanishes on one extremal trajectory.

The legal canonical Huber instance in accepted0be8b54e0a33381c, with
x_0=1, x_star=0, threshold 1/B_n, gives 2f_n=1/B_n for this physical word.
Consequently the constructed upper bound is attained: W_n(d^(n))=1/B_n.

Some explicit horizons also check the orientation and normalization. Put
rho=1+sqrt(2).

* n=1: d=(3/2), B_1=4, u=(2,2), L_(1,0)=2.
  Thus lambda_(1,0)=lambda_(0,*)=lambda_(1,*)=1 and the remainder is
  ||x_0-2g_0-2g_1||^2/4.
* n=2: put z=sqrt(9+8sqrt(2)). The maximizing split is k=1 and
  d=(sqrt(2),(3+z)/4), B_2=(5+4sqrt(2)+z)/2.
  The weights are u=(sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),(1+z)/2).
  The ONLY finite entries are L_(0,1)=1, L_(1,0)=rho,
  L_(2,1)=2rho. The full multipliers are 2L/B_2 and 2u/B_2.
* n=3: the maximizing split is k=1,
  d=(sqrt(2),1+sqrt(2),3/2), B_3=6+4sqrt(2),
  u=(sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2)).
  The ONLY finite entries are
  L_(0,1)=1, L_(1,0)=rho, L_(2,1)=rho,
  L_(3,1)=rho, L_(3,2)=rho^2.

Here the small split comparisons can themselves be checked exactly. At n=2,
G(1,4)=4+2sqrt(3)<15/2, whereas G(rho,1)>15/2: use
sqrt(2)>45/32 and sqrt(2)>7/5 to obtain
sqrt(9+8sqrt(2))>9/2 and the latter strict bound. At n=3, B_2<8 since
rho<5/2 and G(5/2,1)=(11+sqrt(21))/2<8. Thus
G(1,B_2)<G(1,8)=6+4sqrt(2). Also
P_2=(2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2)))/2<(4+3sqrt(2))/2,
because (2+2sqrt(2))^2=12+8sqrt(2)>10+8sqrt(2).
The inverse relation G(A,1)=M iff A=(M-sqrt(M))/2 shows
G(P_2,1)<6+4sqrt(2), while G(rho,4)=6+4sqrt(2).

## 5. A second exact tight instance and full-array implications

For a primitive source word of mass A,

    product_i |1-h_i|=1/A.                            (Q-P)

Indeed it is 1 for the empty word. At a primitive join, put C=Omega(A,E)
and d=b-1. Then C=A+E+d and dC=AE, so the joined product is
d/(AE)=1/C. Similarly the dominant source word satisfies

    product_i |1-h_i|=1/sqrt(B_n).                    (Q-D)

The dominant induction is d/(A sqrt(D)) with d=A/r and B_n=Dr^2.
The actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, therefore gives exactly
2f_n=product_i(1-h_i)^2=1/B_n. It is a second normalized tight instance.

Any full certificate, however its multipliers and rank are reselected, obeys
for a changed nonnegative word h'=h+e

    q B_n >= max{B_n/(B_n+2sum e_i),
                 product_i(1+e_i/(h_i-1))^2}.        (Two tests)

The second expression is valid without assuming h'_i>1 because it is
squared; when logs are used below require h'_i>1. The first is the canonical
Huber bound with the TRUE new step sum, the second the actual unit quadratic.
In particular qB_n<=1-delta, delta in (0,1), requires

    sum e_i >= delta B_n/[2(1-delta)],
    sum log(1+e_i/(h_i-1)) <= log(1-delta)/2           (if h'_i>1).

For a differentiable one-sided continuation h(t),q(t) at t=0 this gives

    q'(0) >= -2 B_n^(-2) sum h_i'(0),
    q'(0) >= (2/B_n) sum h_i'(0)/(h_i-1).            (Tangent tests)

These are NECESSARY only. At the exact baseline, complementarity on the
Huber instance forces every optimum-source multiplier lambda_(*,i) to be
zero, for n>=1: Q_(*,i)=tau(tau-x_i)<0, since s(h)>0 and
x_i>tau. This conclusion applies to every tight baseline certificate,
not merely the particular source array. It does NOT force those entries
to remain zero after a perturbation with non-Huber worst-case behavior.

## 6. Exact obstruction for a relaxed one-fan dominant join

This class changes the source coupling and bridge, including signed
perturbations of every positive parameter within their final sign constraints.
It is strictly more general than fixing b=psi or fixing the rank-one remainder.
It still retains a specified interface and a single cross fan; it is not the
full cone of arbitrary cross-copy interpolation edges.

Take ANY primitive left certificate of the form

    E_L+sum L_L Q = R_L,

and ANY sharp dominant right certificate of the form

    E_R+sum L_R Q = R_R,

where E_L and E_R have the definitions above, D=2y+1, sum v_i=D,
the child finite arrays are nonnegative, and R_L,R_R are full PSD quadratics.
The steps and arrays inside either child need not be their source selections.
Allow an arbitrary bridge b>=0 and parameters

    gamma>=0, lambda>=mu>=0, r=lambda-mu,
    mu D=x+gamma.                                   (Flow)

The new optimum-sink weights and finite array are

    u=(h_left,gamma,r v),
    L=L_L+lambda L_R+mu sum v_i E_(i,p),
    M=x+gamma+rD>0.

Positive child weights and multipliers may be independently reselected so
long as their displayed child identities remain true. No sign restriction
is placed on their DERIVATIVES; only the final nonnegative arrays are required.
Let V=sum_{right} v_i g_i and A=x+1. Direct expansion on the original space
gives the exact identity

    ||x_0||^2/2 + sum u_i Q_i* - M f_z + sum L_ij Q_ij
    = ||x_p-gamma g_p-rV||^2/2
      + R_L+lambda R_R
      + (K_11||g_p||^2+2K_12<g_p,V>+K_22||V||^2)/2, (Join)

where the ENTIRE mixed block is

    K_11=A^2-(gamma-1)^2,
    K_22=lambda-r^2,
    K_12=lambda b-mu-gamma r.                        (K)

To verify the identity, use

    sum v_i Q_i* = sum v_i Q_(i,p)
                  +D(f_p-||g_p||^2/2)+<V,g_p-x_p>,

multiply the right certificate by lambda, subtract mu times this identity,
add the left certificate and gamma(Q_p*-f_z), and substitute
x_(p+1)=x_p-bg_p. The coefficient of f_p-f_z is
x+gamma-mu D and hence is zero. Completing the indicated square leaves
x(x+1)+mu D+gamma-gamma^2=A^2-(gamma-1)^2,
lambda-r^2, and lambda b-mu-gamma r as claimed.

The condition K PSD is necessary for the FULL parent quadratic to be PSD,
even if both child remainders have arbitrarily large ranks. Here is why this
is stronger than an unsupported endpoint compression. Put all left formal
gradients, INCLUDING g_p, equal to an arbitrary vector a, and all right
gradients equal to an independent arbitrary vector c. Choose affine function
values separately on each child: f_i=f_child_end+(remaining child step sum)
times its constant gradient norm squared. All within-child Q vanish. Direct
substitution gives E_L=0 and E_R=D(2y+1-D)||c||^2/2=0. Thus
R_L=R_R=0 on this restriction for EVERY such child certificate. Moreover
V=Dc, and choosing x_0=(x+gamma)a+rDc makes the first square zero.
All choices of a,c are still available, so the restriction is precisely K.
This is a legitimate test of a full formal PSD matrix; it does not claim these
independent constants are one legal primal trajectory.

It follows that

    gamma<=A+1,
    r^2-r-(x+gamma)/D<=0.

Let w=x+gamma. Then w<=2A and

    M=w+rD <= w+(D+sqrt(D^2+4Dw))/2 <= G(A,D).        (Mass cap)

Both scalar expressions are strictly increasing in w>=0. Equality forces

    gamma=A+1, mu=2A/D,
    r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, lambda=r^2.

At equality both diagonals of K are zero. PSD then forces the mixed entry
to vanish too, giving b=(mu+gamma r)/lambda=1+A/r=psi(x,y).
Thus the exact source mass is the UNIQUE maximizing local parameter choice
within this relaxed join, including its bridge. A positive extra remainder
does not buy a larger mass because the independently constant child modes
annihilate every sharp child remainder.

This is a finite inequality, not an infinitesimal one. Apply it at any number
of dominant nodes with primitive prefix sums at most S_circle of their
lengths and sharp dominant children. At a node of total length n, the exact
DP monotonicity gives M<=G(P_k,B_l)<=B_n inductively. The root need only have
the displayed valid certificate of level 1/M; it need not itself be sharp.
Consequently this entire recursive one-fan family cannot certify
q B_n<1 at ANY horizon. It allows changes at unboundedly many scales and a
changed bridge rule, so its obstruction is not just the fixed finite
terminal-library result. Its retained hypotheses are the sharp child
interfaces, the physical primitive prefix weights, and the single cross fan.
Non-Huber children, independent history weights, extra interior cross edges,
and couplings outside this construction remain open.

## 7. A separate global schedule class, with unrestricted full re-selection

Let n>=1, h=d^(n), and a_i=h_i-1>0. Let T be ANY n-by-n doubly stochastic
matrix (nonnegative entries; every row and column sums to one), and c>=0.
Define a new actual word in its new chronological order by

    h'_i=1+c(Ta)_i.                                  (Mix)

Then

    W_n(h')>=1/B_n.                                  (Mix obstruction)

No hypothesis whatsoever is made here on its certificate array, its rank,
or its worst-case function. This class includes every permutation, arbitrary
mixing of different scales, and any common dilation or contraction of the
positive step gaps; most such words are not ConPD words.

Proof. Since column sums of T equal one,
s(h')=n+c sum a_i. If 0<=c<=1, the actual Huber lower bound is at least
1/(1+2s(h))=1/B_n. If c>=1, log concavity gives

    sum_i log((Ta)_i)
      >=sum_(i,j) T_ij log(a_j)=sum_j log(a_j).

The unit quadratic therefore gives

    W_n(h') >= c^(2n) product_i((Ta)_i)^2
             >= c^(2n) product_i a_i^2=c^(2n)/B_n.

This proves the finite, uniform claim. It does not exclude making the
normalized gap vector more dispersed, or incomparable in the majorization
order, nor any schedule with some entries below one.

## 8. What is still blocked

The original target asks for either a uniform matching all-schedule lower
bound, or one fixed delta>0 and a certified finite continuation with
q_n B_n<=1-delta on an explicit unbounded sequence. Neither follows here.

The all-horizon source word and full array are now explicit. The one-fan
closure and global gap mixing classes have exact finite obstructions.
The remaining useful variation must leave those classes. Passing (Two tests)
or (Tangent tests) alone is not a PSD continuation. In particular positive
optimum-source multipliers and a higher-rank remainder after deformation
are allowed by the original problem and must not be excluded by inference
from sharp baseline complementarity.

No verification authority was added by this reconstruction. This file and
all of its new conclusions remain draft evidence until selected verification.

This is an UNVERIFIED analytical audit observation, not a fact or formal verdict. For u=7/4 and theta=1/u=4/7, the specified scalar polynomial trajectory has p0=1 and p1=p2=p3=p4=0. Its exact all-value minimum follows directly from all ordered interpolation rows: f1=f2=f3=f4, f0-f4>=theta^2/2, with equality attained by the Part I conjugate construction (or F(x)=0 for x<=0; F(x)=x^2/2 on[0,theta]; F(x)=theta*x-theta^2/2 for x>=theta, shifted so F(1)-F(0)=theta-theta^2/2 would be the upper value, so use the ramp located at1-theta instead: F(x)=0 for x<=1-theta, F(x)=(x-1+theta)^2/2 on[1-theta,1], and F(x)=theta*(x-1)+theta^2/2 for x>=1). Then V=u*theta^2+u*(1-u)*theta^2=u*(2-u)*theta^2=1/7. Formula(I.13) instead asserts V>=u^2*theta^3*(2-u*theta)=4/7. In the already correct general recurrence(I.11), setting S=0,t=u gives E1=u^2*theta^3*(2-u^2*theta), not its displayed specialization. On0<theta<=1/u this correct bracket is at least2-u=1/4>0, so the scalar positivity proof is locally repairable. Section5a repeats the same incorrect specialization. The exact word and all other audit items remain under independent review; no package PASS is implied.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001377 -->
# Independent analytical audit of the final C107 threshold package

Status: UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL AUDIT, not a formal-verifier verdict or a
new accepted fact. Auditor max4, round45, independent of author max3.
All seven assigned interfaces survive the analytical attacks recorded
below. No correction to the selected text was required by this audit.
This is not an assertion of seed existence or original-goal completion.

The selected object is the COMPLETE public finding0b088b5d300e42a1:

`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/0b088b5d300e42a1.md`

Its SHA256, checked before this audit, is
`90db94b51972f03214e19e2ac8abef3eb73394871943d95392713075c68540a0`.
The original file is preserved, without edits. The hash identifies the
audited text and is not mathematical evidence.

The ONLY accepted correctness input is fact04219554e41d9750. I read its
exact statement and proof Sections2--3 and7--13. In particular, the
general-length seed construction appears explicitly in accepted
Sections10--13; it is not inferred from the four-step headline alone.
No other shared draft, author's assertion, numerical experiment, source
hash, or earlier audit is used as a mathematical premise here.

TASK.md and allocation guidance8abd66a4a086a9fa authorize one analytical
round after completed44, with completed ceiling45 and attempted ceiling46
including the historical interruption. No computation, DP, solver,
machine algebra, interval replay, formal-verifier call, consult, native
subagent, installation or resource renewal was used. All arithmetic
below is written-out symbolic/rational reasoning. Reading files,
operational hashing and persistence do not evaluate new benchmark data.

## Definitions and accepted interfaces

For a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over finite-dimensional convex
differentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star,
under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), with L>0. This is W=2R.

For a,d>=0 define

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda).

Every split uses nonnegative integer k,l; no split is removed. Accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the following precise ingredients:

* Section2: U_N=C_(N-1) for N>=1, U_0=0,
  U_(2a)=lambda U_a and U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)). These concern C only.
* Sections3 and7: 1<rho<4/3, B_n>=C_n, B is increasing, and the positive
  finite limit Z_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) exists for every integer
  b>=1, with lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1)<=Z_b for every integer j>=0.
* Sections8--9: the specific lower endpoint for B_3071 quoted in item3,
  and the explicit convergence error quoted in item5.
* Sections10--13: the full general-length primitive seed condition,
  physical joins, one-untouched-leaf substitution, and shifted-ray
  consequence quoted in items6--7.

Write Gamma_5=7977607/806000 and Gamma_6=387153999/32240000 in this
audit; these abbreviate C107's Gamma5_c107 and Gamma6_c107. Write
q_j=(2/3)^j. The new claims under attack are

    Z_18/Z_3<Gamma_5<99/10,
    Z_21/Z_3<Gamma_6<1201/100,

and their conditional finite consequences for ACTUAL full length-five
or length-six primitive seeds. The fixed ray is b=3 throughout.

## 1. Original recurrence, normalization and all-split comparison

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK, including both zero arguments and
the larger-root condition. C107 preserves the accepted W=2R convention
and the original C and B recurrences.

When a=d=0, G(a,lambda d)=lambda Omega(a,d)=0. Otherwise set
w=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w. The positive-root identity gives

    0<=t<=1,
    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).

The polynomial P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 has larger root
G(a,lambda d), since its discriminant is lambda^2 d^2+8lambda ad.
Using lambda^2=2lambda+1, the substitution is exactly

    P(lambda w)/w^2
      =[t/(1+t)]*[2lambda^2-4lambda+(4-4lambda)t+4t^2]
      =[t/(1+t)]*[2-4sqrt(2)t+4t^2]
      =4t(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t)>=0.

The branch check is essential and valid: w>=a+d, and

    lambda(a+d)-(2a+lambda d/2)
       =(lambda-2)a+lambda d/2>=0.

Thus lambda w lies to the right of the vertex, so P(lambda w)>=0
places it at or beyond the larger root, not below the smaller one.
Consequently G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d).

The direct boundary checks agree. For a=0 the two sides equal lambda d;
for d=0 they are 2a and lambda a. The nonzero equality case also survives:
take a=1, d=3sqrt(2)-4>0. Then w=sqrt(2), lambda d=2-sqrt(2), and
sqrt((2-sqrt(2))^2+8(2-sqrt(2)))=3sqrt(2)-2>0. Therefore both
sides are 2+sqrt(2). This tests the internal zero of the factored
polynomial as well as the correct square-root branch.

Both displayed kernels are coordinatewise nondecreasing on the
nonnegative quadrant, including the axes. Starting from B_0<=lambda C_0,
each original split obeys

    G(C_k,B_l)<=G(C_k,lambda C_l)
       <=lambda Omega(C_k,C_l)<=lambda C_n.

Taking the maximum establishes B_n<=lambda C_n without selecting an
optimizer of B. Accepted symmetric dyadic scaling gives
C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1); dividing and taking the accepted limit
therefore yields Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1).

The new comparison is not assumed in the accepted fact, whose
Section9 used the weaker B_n<=4C_n. It has been rederived here.

## 2. Discrete primitive identities and every rational enclosure

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK. The dyadic identities are accepted
inputs; all five radical-enclosure inequalities are new and checked here.

Let c,p,u,v,w abbreviate respectively the candidate's c107,p107,u107,
v107,w107. The leaf-count identities give, with no B balancing assumption,

    U_3=c=Omega(1,lambda)=C_2,
    U_5=p=Omega(lambda,c)=C_4,
    U_9=u=Omega(lambda^2,p)=C_8,
    U_11=v=Omega(p,lambda c)=C_10,
    U_21=w=Omega(lambda p,v)=C_20.

Indeed U_4=lambda^2, U_6=lambda c, U_10=lambda p and
U_18=lambda u. In particular C_17=lambda u and

    Z_18<=lambda^2 u,  Z_21<=lambda w.

The lambda enclosure follows from 49<50 and 9801>9800:

    7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70,
    12/5<lambda<169/70,
    lambda^2=2lambda+1<204/35.

the correct root branch and strict positivity. D is the positive common
denominator for P, and N is its integer numerator.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| c: (1,169/70) | 502/125 | 5265/8750 | 1093750 | 2405 |
| p: (169/70,502/125) | 1923/250 | 2208/1750 | 437500 | 4084 |

| w: (324987/17500,419/20) | 23841/500 | 142823/17500 | 8750000 | 804318 |

Here are independent full numerator expansions, in table order:

    17640280-14997250-2640625=2405,
    25885503-21639519-4241900=4084,
    11595430-9630027-1961460=3943,
    7680793750-6374791700-1305147792=854258,
    19893764835-16488721692-3404238825=804318.

For clarity, the common-denominator multipliers for the three terms
x^2, (a+d)x, ad are respectively

    c: 70,125,15625, using denominators15625,8750,70;

    (169/70)(1923/250)=324987/17500.

All x-a-d, D and N in the table are positive. Hence each x exceeds

monotonicity, applied in this order, gives all five strict upper
enclosures, including u<407/25 and w<23841/500. No floating-point
radical estimate enters this argument.

The separate lower estimate c>4 also holds. The defining polynomial
has value 12-5lambda<0 at4, and has one negative and one positive root;

## 3. Accepted endpoint, exact index, depth and scaling

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK within the accepted endpoint's

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 Sections8--9 states exactly

## 4. Both strict Gamma bounds and the historical comparison constants

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK. The comparisons establish smaller
sufficient upper thresholds; they do not order the true b=1 and b=3
ratios or certify the older drafts.

Writing t=sqrt(2), multiplication gives

    lambda^4=17+12t, lambda^5=41+29t, lambda^6=99+70t,
    lambda^10=(41+29t)^2=3363+2378t,
    lambda^12=(99+70t)^2=19601+13860t.

Since 1-t=-lambda^(-1), the conjugates of these even powers are their
strictly positive reciprocals. Therefore

    lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726,
    lambda^12+lambda^(-12)=39202,
    lambda^10<6726, lambda^12<39202.

Also

    lambda^11<6726*(169/70)=1136694/70<16239,

where 16239*70-1136694=36>0. All factors subsequently divided by are
positive. Combining items1--3 gives

    Z_18/Z_3<lambda^12 u/64480
       <39202*(407/25)/64480
        =15955214/1612000=7977607/806000=Gamma_5,

    Z_21/Z_3<lambda^11 w/64480
       <16239*(23841/500)/64480
        =387153999/32240000=Gamma_6.

The exact products in the second line are
23841*16000=381456000 and 23841*239=5697999, summing to387153999.
The capacity gaps are

    99/10-Gamma_5=(7979400-7977607)/806000=1793/806000>0,
    1201/100-Gamma_6
       =(387202400-387153999)/32240000=48401/32240000>0.

The comparisons to the two historical b=1 numbers are only arithmetic:

    292666/29275-99/10=(2926660-2898225)/292750
       =28435/292750>0,
    90/7-1201/100=(9000-8407)/700=593/700>0.

No old b=1 sufficiency proof is required to establish these inequalities.
Ordering two upper enclosures supplies no ordering of the underlying
true ratios, and C107 expressly avoids that invalid inference.

## 5. Uniform denominator-ray error and positivity before division

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK for every stated j; division is
made only after the required lower bound is positive.

For n_j=3*2^j-1, accepted Section9 gives

    0<=Z_3-lambda^(-j)B_(n_j)<=E(3,j),
    E(3,j)=8sqrt(2)*3^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
       +(3/25)*4^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
       +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

The coefficient bounds are all strict and oriented correctly:
sqrt(2)<3/2; 3^(rho-1)<3^(1/3)<3/2 because rho<4/3 and
3<27/8; and 4^(rho/2)=2^rho=lambda<5/2. Also sqrt(lambda)>3/2.
For every integer j>=0, each of 2^(-j), lambda^(-j/2), lambda^(-j)
is at most q_j=(2/3)^j. Thus

    E(3,j)<18*2^(-j)+(3/10)*lambda^(-j/2)
                       +(1/1000)*lambda^(-j)
           <=(18301/1000)q_j<19q_j.

The replacement 9/10000<1/1000 is in the upper-bound direction.
The endpoint from item3 gives

    Z_3>64480/6726>19/2,

since 128960-127794=1166>0. It follows that

    lambda^(-j)B_(n_j)>=Z_3-E(3,j)
       >Z_3-19q_j>Z_3(1-2q_j).                 (A)

This inequality is valid even at j=0 when the last expression is
negative; that case is not used as a positive denominator. Independently,
accepted B>=C and the exact primitive scaling give

    B_(n_j)>=lambda^j c>4lambda^j.             (B)

The finite comparison in item7 explicitly restricts to q_j<1/3.
Then 1-2q_j>1/3, 1-q_j/2>5/6 and 1-3q_j>0. All divisions by the
lower bounds are therefore legitimate. The general starting depth
imposes the still stronger q_j<=epsilon/6<1/6.

## 6. Actual full seed and physical substitution interface

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK as a conditional statement about
the complete original arrays. No actual seed has been supplied.

Let r be5 or6, h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1))>=0, s=sum h_i and A=1+s>Gamma_r.
Both Gamma values exceed1, so A>1. The required multipliers are every
Lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in{0,...,r}, with Lambda_ii=0. They number
r(r+1), namely30 for r=5 and42 for r=6. With e the all-ones column,

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2>=0 on R^(r+1).

These are exactly accepted Section10 for a general length r. To check
the signs and orientation explicitly, normalize L=1, put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
         +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Here f_i=f(x_i), g_i=grad f(x_i), I is the identity matrix,
out_i=sum_j Lambda_ij and in_i=sum_j Lambda_ji.
The coefficient of f_i in sum Lambda_ij Q_ij is out_i-in_i,
which equals (L_flow^T e)_i. Thus the displayed omega cancels the
function-value coefficients of V_h. The position terms contribute
sym(L_flow(T-I)), where sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2. The squared differences
contribute sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Their sum with V_h's diagonal
is precisely M_prim: the nonterminal entries become h_i(2-h_i),
and the terminal entry becomes -s(s+2). Hence

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Here Gram is the matrix of all gradient inner products and the matrix
pairing is the entrywise inner product. Accepted Section10 gives
Q_ij<=0 on actual trajectories, so full PSD and all nonnegative
multiplier signs imply V_h>=0. All r+1 formal gradients remain, even
when h_i=0. No duplicated-position quotient, rank assumption, support
restriction or zero-remainder assumption enters this implication.

For a primitive child its capacity is one plus its step sum; for a
dominant child its mass is one plus twice its step sum. A_left,A_right
denote the primitive child capacities, and D_right the dominant right
child mass. Accepted Sections11--12 give the actual physical joins:

* A primitive join uses bridge1+d with
  d(A_left+A_right+d)=A_left*A_right; its capacity is Omega(A_left,A_right).
* A dominant join uses bridge1+A_left/zeta, with
  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2; its mass is G(A_left,D_right).

All capacities and masses here are positive. Those sections retain
the original interpolation coordinates and arbitrary child PSD
remainders, so the stated full seed is a valid input for either length.

In a fully expanded B_n tree there are n bridge nodes, n primitive
empty leaves and exactly one dominant empty leaf. This follows from
the split k+l=n-1: C_k contributes k+1 primitive leaves, B_l contributes
l primitive leaves and its one dominant leaf. Substitution of a
length-r seed adds nr physical steps; the n original bridges remain.
The last dominant leaf stays empty. Thus the actual length is (r+1)n.
Call the resulting physical word Hsub_n and put Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n.

Homogeneity multiplies each primitive branch capacity by A. The
auxiliary scalar operation of also changing the last dominant leaf
from mass1 toA would give root mass A B_n. The actual word does not
make that change. Accepted Section12 propagates its square-root loss
by the increasing, 1-Lipschitz right-mass map and yields

    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(Dsub_n)<=sqrt(A B_n),
    Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

The lower square-root bound is positive since B_n>=1. The same
accepted section gives the full all-function certificate and matching
Huber witness, so

    W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n.

Consequently there is no missing massA at the final leaf and no
unjustified use of a scalar capacity surrogate for an actual word.

## 7. True offsets, finite starting depth and uniform margin

Verdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK for every stated depth and every
A above its stated convenient threshold. No finite-depth flaw or
failure of the eventual implication was found.

With n_j=3*2^j-1, the actual output length is

    N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1).

The output ray b'=3(r+1) has offset d=r in accepted Section9:
N_j=b'2^j-1-r. Thus the lengths are18*2^j-6 for r=5 and21*2^j-7
for r=6, and the accepted shifted limit is exactly Z_(3(r+1)).
For the finite upper comparison, accepted monotonicity and the
LOWER ray enclosure give the correctly oriented bound

    B_(N_j)<=B_(3(r+1)2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(3(r+1)).       (C)

There is no removal of the finite offset from the actual horizon.

For every A>Gamma_r, item5(B) and A>1 show

    0<=ell_(n_j)<1/sqrt(B_(n_j))
       <(1/2)lambda^(-j/2)<=(1/2)q_j.

This bound is uniform as A grows: the factor1-A^(-1/2) remains below1.
Together with (A), (C), the actual substitution bound and the strict
ray-ratio enclosure, it gives, for q_j<1/3,

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
      <(Gamma_r/A)/[(1-2q_j)(1-q_j/2)^2]
      <=(Gamma_r/A)/(1-3q_j).                          (D)

The polynomial denominator check is

    (1-2q)(1-q/2)^2-(1-3q)
       =q^2(9/4-q/2)>=0 for0<=q<1/3.

Positivity needed for the reciprocal comparison was checked in item5.

Define epsilon=1-Gamma_r/A. It lies strictly between0 and1. For

    j>=ceil(log(6/epsilon)/log(3/2)),

one has q_j<=epsilon/6<1/6, so the condition q_j<1/3 in (D) is
automatically satisfied. In fact this starting depth is at least5,
since (3/2)^4=81/16<6<6/epsilon. Now

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
       <(1-epsilon)/(1-epsilon/2)<1-epsilon/2.

For the last strict inequality, right side minus left side is
epsilon^2/[4(1-epsilon/2)]>0. Since U_(N_j)^ZJ=1/B_(N_j)>0, this
is exactly the candidate's stated normalized W bound.

At the two convenient capacities, the relative margins are

    epsilon_5=1793/7979400>1/8000,
    epsilon_6=48401/387202400>1/8000.

The strict products are

    1793*8000=14344000>7979400,
    48401*8000=387208000=387202400+5600.

Since Gamma_r>0, the function1-Gamma_r/A increases with A, so these
are lower bounds for ALL A>=99/10 or A>=1201/100, respectively.
Finally,

    (3/2)^10=59049/1024>57, since59049-58368=681>0,
    (3/2)^7=2187/128>17, since2187-2176=11>0,
    (3/2)^27>57^2*17=3249*17=55233>48000.

Therefore every j>=27 satisfies q_j<1/48000<epsilon/6. The general
finite bound gives the strict factor1-1/16000 at the two TRUE output
horizons, uniformly in A above the respective convenient threshold.

For completeness, the accepted general-length limit in Section13,
or the accepted shifted enclosure and the square-root loss in item6,
also gives

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) -> Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3)<1.

Thus both the eventual implication and the stronger finite-depth
claim survive this audit separately.

## Scope, counterexample status and handoff

Every assigned new load-bearing calculation has been addressed above.
There is no identified mathematical refutation and no missing check
within these seven interfaces. The explicit axis cases and nonzero
equality case in item1 are non-refuting tests, not counterexamples.
No local repair is proposed because none was needed in the audited
text. This conclusion remains an independent ANALYTICAL assessment;
only a later explicitly authorized fact_submit could create a fact.

The accepted finite DP certificate was NOT recomputed or independently
replayed. Its exact B_3071 lower endpoint is used in its already
accepted scope, as TASK explicitly allows. The rest of the accepted
fact, unrelated old drafts and unallocated seed constructions are not
claimed to have been audited afresh.

The package does not provide an actual length-five or length-six seed,
does not prove nonexistence of one, does not optimize over phases and
does not compare the true b=1 and b=3 ratios. The thresholds are
sufficient upper enclosures for a future full seed, not necessity
barriers for arbitrary algorithms. There is no original all-n/all-h
profile result here; W=2R and the original Goal remain unchanged and OPEN.

This completes the ONE selected max4 audit round. No fact was submitted,
no accepted fact was created, and no further research round is inferred
from this handoff. An audit completion is not evidence of process exit;
the outer loop owns its actual completion counter and terminal state.

## 8. A double-zero root really survives: an actual legal scope counterexample

It would be false to extend the annihilation conclusion from a simple zero
root to a double zero root, or to assume that the terminal constant gradient
has nonnegative correlation with the eliminated component. The counterexample
below uses the ACTUAL critical word and has a unique minimizer and distance1.
It is the already verified single-cut mechanism in b98bb807d514e80d, written
explicitly in the new growing Newton coordinates to audit their scope.

Let S_total=sum H_m, d=d_0 and T_tail=S_total-d. Define

 A_tail=(d-1)/(2T_tail+1)>0,
 f'(x)=max(-A_tail,min(1,x)), f(0)=0, x_0=1.           (39)

This derivative is monotone, continuous and1-Lipschitz, so its integral is
one convex1-smooth function with unique minimizer0. The first query has
gradient1; after the actual first step d the point is

 x_1=1-d=-A_tail(2T_tail+1).

Every later query stays at most -A_tail(T_tail+1)<-A_tail, since the remaining
total step mass is T_tail. Thus every later gradient is the constant -A_tail.
At the actual end of H_m the full normalized payoff is exactly

             2f(x_n)=(d-1)^2/(2T_tail+1)>0.            (40)

In fact this also REFUTES extension of the super-small bound (28) to a double
zero root, for every m>=128. Count the levels to obtain

 1+S_total=A_mass c^m+(1-A_mass)2^m,
 A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).

Here A_mass<1 follows from ac<1=lambda(lambda-2)<c(c-2). Hence S_total<c^m,
and (10) implies

                  2f(x_n)>(3/64)c^(-m).               (40a)

This exceeds100000 exp(-m^3/4): logc<1 and
log(100000*64/3)<15, while m^3/4-m>15 for m>=128.
Thus the zero-root multiplicity in Section7 is quantitatively load-bearing,
not only needed for the exact-zero statement in Section3.

All ordered constraints, including every queried value and the optimum, hold
because these are actual jets of the single explicitly defined function (39).

This transcript belongs to the growing class

              chi=z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j),     (41)

of degree m+2. To specify the real map T on the quotient, define it directly
on a polynomial p by

 T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+d w p(mu_0),
 v=-A_tail, w=1+A_tail, u=1-dw.                       (42)

Evaluation at0, the derivative at0 and evaluation atmu_0 all vanish on
multiples of chi, so this is a well-defined quotient map. The remaining
level coordinates are allowed but have zero image. For every i,

 x_i=u-s_i v+d w P_i(mu_0),
 g_i=v+w P_i(mu_0),  s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.

P_0(mu_0)=1 and P_i(mu_0)=0 for every i>=1, because the first step is d_0.
Thus these formulas reproduce exactly (39), and T[1]=u+dw=1. The common
metric T^T T is real PSD; no nonlinear or time-dependent map has been used.
In particular v*(dw)=-A_tail d(1+A_tail)<0. Hence the tempting cross-term
inequality <v,eliminated_component>>=0 is already false on legal data.

The function is genuinely nonquadratic on the transcript: a quadratic with
minimum at0 and g_0=1 atx_0=1 would have curvature1, while its gradient atx_1
would be1-d, not -A_tail. For large positive T_tail these differ. Its exact
payoff is the known weak first-cut bound, so (40) is NOT an improved
adversary or a profile resolution. It proves only that a double-zero
residual and its negative cross terms must be handled in any extension of
Sections3 or7. Arbitrary shifted upper-level nodes, double-zero blocks and
changing generators remain open after these scoped obstructions.

# Original-row completion of the positive-gap constraint

Status: complete joint mathematical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4
round32, the second and last analytical round allocated by TASK.md and
master_guidance6e0a18d700dcd62f after completed30. All numerical, model,
solver, replay, formal-verifier, consult and subordinate-agent allowances
remain closed. No such operation is used here.

The accepted input is fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the ORIGINAL
primitive matrix identity. The unverified round31 draft8e9bbdb33b32a081
motivated this work but is not an accepted premise. All identities used
below are rederived in this joint argument.

The actual positive-gap compatibility problem is not solved globally.
The new results are: an original coefficient sign contradicts the old
strictly capped relaxation equality ray; its quantitative version removes
that artificial cap entirely; the resulting original-row remainder has a
strictly positive first-row energy coefficient; the actual full quadratic
column and terminal row give an additional quantitative near-equality
test; and every original coefficient on the NEW sharp equality locus is
matched explicitly. No high-capacity member of that locus, and no word
surviving the COMPLETE older necessary system and violating a new test,
is asserted. The final comparison still missing is stated precisely.

## 1. Original full interface and common identities

Let

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v),  a,b,c>0, v>=0, bc<=1,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, H0pre=1+a+b,
    s=sum h, C=1+s=4+a+b+c+v,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t), z=a*b*v*t,
    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,
    D=N^2*b^2-A^2.

Let e_i be the five original standard columns, indexed0 through4,
e=sum e_i, and P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Define

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    Dmat=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=Dmat+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The hypotheses are ALL twenty signs Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, zero
diagonal, L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on ALL of R^5. There is no support,
rank, symmetry, reversal, or duplicated-gradient restriction.

The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                 -s(s+1)||g4||^2, (1)

on formal data with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Formal substitutions below
are used as polynomial identities; no unproved interpolation is assumed.

Te=e+P and the flow give e^TMe=0. Indeed e^TDmat e=-s^2-sum h_i^2
and omega^T(e+P)=s^2+sum h_i^2. Applying PSD to e+r*y for both
signs of the real number r gives Me=0. The same argument proves

    |<x,y>_M|^2<=||x||_M^2*||y||_M^2,
    ||x||_M^2=0 implies Mx=0,                           (2)

where <x,y>_M=x^TMy and ||x||_M^2=x^TMx. Under the flow, the
individual kernel equations are

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,
    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)

These follow by expanding 2Me=2Dmat e+L(e+P)+T^T omega.

Put

    E=e0, J=e0+e1, B=e3+e4,
    m=||E||_M^2, S=||J||_M^2, Tcut=||B||_M^2, rJB=<J,B>_M,
    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,
    Avec=u*E-a*beta*J-a*b*mu*B,
    c03old=u*mu*z, c23old=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.

The three exact identities needed here are

    R=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*||Avec||_M^2
         +ell*mu*(Hpre*B0+beta*B1)+c03old*Lambda03+c23old*Lambda23, (4)

    D=beta*((b*w*H0pre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*rJB-beta*S
                     -u*Lambda20-b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)),       (5)

    2<E,B>_M=a*B0-Lambda03-(1+v)*Lambda04.                         (6)

To prove (4), use saturated values f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 on

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

For these values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In ell times the base
plus mu times the auxiliary, the complete nonzero residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-c03old, Q23=-c23old.

Every other ordered residual is zero. In particular Q13 cancels
because ell=a^2*b*(1-bt); at Q03 use ell+ab(1+abt)=ab*u, and
at Q23 use ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. Telescoping (1) gives
R; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).

For (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients
(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine
values through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column
modulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are
Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are
Q03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.
Thus (1) gives

 D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))
                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).

Besides (6), direct multiplication of T gives

    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.

Adding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).

The additional ORIGINAL row used this round is

    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)

In fact e2=e-J-B and
2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)
without any PSD quotient or new support assumption.

## 2. Why the old strictly capped equality ray cannot match

For reference, the earlier three-vector expansion used a positive k
and theta with

    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta+(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4
                                                     -k*ell*mu^2,
    x=b*w-a*theta/2,
    Z=z-(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta/2.

Here is the expansion, without importing the earlier draft as a premise.
Combining (4)--(6) and expanding two squares gives

 kR-D=||beta*J+x*B||_M^2+k*mu*||Avec-theta*B/(2*k*mu)||_M^2
       -F(k,theta)*Tcut
       +[k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*u*theta/2]*B0
       +beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w)*B1+beta*u*Lambda20
       +[k*c03old-u*theta/2]*Lambda03
       +[beta*b*v*c-u*(1+v)*theta/2]*Lambda04
       +beta*b*v*c*Lambda14+k*c23old*Lambda23.

For v>0, take the unique root obtained by minimizing F in
0<=theta<=Ucap:=2*beta*z/(a*u). The root lies strictly between
k0 and kold, defined below: the unrestricted minimum is already
positive at k0 and a small positive theta makes F negative at kold.
The restricted minimizing theta is min(theta(k),Ucap). Every displayed
cost is nonnegative. For the B0 cost use its value u*(1-bc) at
k0,theta=0; the other signs follow from k>k0,
theta<=theta(k)<2*k*mu*z, and theta<=Ucap.

On the strictly capped branch theta=Ucap<theta(k), so F=0 and Z>0.
If kR-D=0 and R>0, the positive costs and the two squares force

    ME=-(Z/u)*MB, MJ=-(x/beta)*MB,
    Lambda20=Lambda03=0.

Here x/beta<1 since bw<=beta and theta>0. Consequently

    M02=(Z/u)*(1-x/beta)*Tcut>0.

The two zero columns also give Tcut>0: otherwise both J and E would
have zero columns and (4), with its positive costs zero, would give
R=0. But the actual original row gives

    2M02=-Lambda02-w*Lambda04<=0.

This is a direct sign contradiction. The old relaxation could select
its Gram energies without satisfying this retained original entry.
The following quantitative argument uses (7) to remove its cap.

## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder

Assume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set

    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).

The minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at

    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).

It is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,
partial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum
is attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is
b^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,
where gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and
the negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.
There is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).

Here is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define

    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta
          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),
    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),
    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,
    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,
    rstar=u*theta/2.

The positive Hbar solves

    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,

which follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also

    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,
    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)
                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)

The inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied
by Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.

Full original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal
diagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.
Every weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence

    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)

We now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.
Define the nonnegative correction

    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)
                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),
    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),
    X=beta*J+x*B.

The EXACT original-array remainder is

    eps:=k*R-D
       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2
         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m
         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02
         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)

where

    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,
    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,
    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,
    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.

To check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is

 beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut
                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.

Subtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.
Restore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in
(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs
from 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the
uncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).

Every displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are
STRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.

Let

    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).

Then

    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)

If qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put
tau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and
Sden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives

    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)
                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),
    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).

The numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality
qcorr<qmax is equivalent to

    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.

Its left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover
2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the
strict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from
tau/(1+tau)<1/2.

Also rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than
(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than
(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).
At k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;
it strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.
Equation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally
rstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.
The definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.

Set

    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,
    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.

The positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is

    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)

In particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. The old
cap theta<=2*beta*z/(a*u) is unnecessary once (7) is retained. On
the old strictly capped branch its root kstar is strictly larger
than this k, so an additional algebraic strip kR<D<=kstar*R is
excluded. This comparison is only with that relaxation, NOT with the
complete older scalar/triangle/curvature conjunction.

## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget

Define

    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).

Tp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives

    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,
    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)
             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)

For the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and
I0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow
gives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and
Mp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains

 pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),
 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,

which gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,
and 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.

By (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define

    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),

omitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with
xi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,

    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)

Subtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients
are nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the
order of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.
The remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is

    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.

Thus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent
energy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity
shows Qplus>=0, and

    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).

That prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not
counted as an independent improvement.

For completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is
xi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)
has supremum

    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

Otherwise its supremum is

    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).

The latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,
the supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is
u*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.
These statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is

 sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).

The earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain
0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where

    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.

Its exact identity is

 Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11
                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)

Here is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set
gfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on

 Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),
 Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).

The nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,
Q12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite
residual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and
<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.
The energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo
e are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).

In particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side
eps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced
by

 min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),
                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)

This follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so
the same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.
It does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,
putting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives

 u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).

For delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply
Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and

    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).

To see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is

 u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)
    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).

The second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting
eta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed
bound. All its hypotheses remain.

## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality

In this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.
Let

    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,
    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,
    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,
    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),
    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),
    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),
    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),
    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).

All denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original
terminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.

Every full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.
Write y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set

 tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y
       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))
                                                            /(2*jcoef),
 Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2
            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.

Then

                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)

Here is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.
The vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is

    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.

The original terminal rows give

    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,
    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.

Since X=Y+alpha E, they imply

 x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M
                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.

Taking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives

 <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut
       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)
       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)

Let eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum
in (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),
 ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).

Also ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and
c14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in
(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use
<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is

 jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)
        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)

For the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give

 R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)
                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C
  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)

The cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of
the SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.
Thus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.
Replacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for
tcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,

 jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.

Its nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases
with tcut>=0, which proves (17).

This supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is
continuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,
and

    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.

Consequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with
Psi(y0)=R, and every full array must have

                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)

Strict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive
coefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition
and proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0
forces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound
(14) alone is zero. This prices a further original cross column.
No uniform lower bound as v,ell,R,Q or other factors vanish is claimed.

## 6. COMPLETE original-array matching on the new equality locus

Assume eps=0 and R>0, still with a,b,c>0,bc<=1,0<v<2. Equation(12)
forces ME=MY=0, hence MX=0, and

    B0=B1=Lambda20=Lambda03=Lambda14=Lambda23=0.

Equation(6) now gives Lambda04=0, and (7) gives Lambda02=0.
The first moment in (3) forces Lambda01=2; node0 flow then gives
Lambda10=2A. Its diagonal is m=1-a^2-2a, so

                         a=sqrt(2)-1, a*A=1.                    (22)

Set

    Tstar=R/rcoef>0, r13=2*rho*Tstar, Bphys=Hpre+w, s=Bphys+v.

On this locus EVERY original multiplier is forced as follows:

    Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2A, Lambda13=r13,
    Lambda12=(2*A*Hpre-(beta+w)*r13)/beta,
    Lambda21=2*Hpre+Lambda12+r13,
    Lambda24=(2*w*N+beta*Lambda21)/(w+v),
    Lambda42=Lambda24/c,
    Lambda32=2*Bphys+r13+(c-1)*Lambda42,
    Lambda43=(2*s*C-(w+v)*Lambda42)/v,
    Lambda34=(1-c)*Lambda42+Lambda43-2*s,                         (23)

and the other TEN off-diagonal coefficients are zero:

    02,03,04,14,20,23,30,31,40,41.

Let

    nstar=Lambda42+Lambda43-s*(s+2),
    vcol=(0,-rho,rho-1,1,0)^T, dcol=(0,0,0,-1,1)^T.

For this array the ENTIRE original matrix is

                  M=Tstar*vcol*vcol^T+nstar*dcol*dcol^T.          (24)

Thus a word on eps=0,R>0 has a full original certificate if and
only if (22) holds and the explicit array (23) has

    Lambda12,Lambda32,Lambda34,Lambda43>=0, nstar>=0.              (25)

All other surviving signs then follow from their displayed formulas.
In particular Lambda21>0, Lambda24>0, Lambda42>0. This is an
actual all-twenty-entry matching, not a supplied covariance-fiber test.

Here is the proof of necessity and sufficiency of these formulas.
From E=0 and X=0 in the M seminorm, MJ=-rho*MB. Equations(4),(8)
give Tcut=Tstar. The original row <e1,B> gives Lambda13=r13.
Node1's moment and flow give respectively Lambda12 and Lambda21
in (23). Since M14=0 and MJ=-rho MB with rho>0, MB has fourth
component zero. Hence M24=0 too, because e2=e-J-B. The original
entry 2M24=c*Lambda42-Lambda24 gives their displayed relation.
Node2's moment and flow give Lambda24 and Lambda32. Node4's
moment and flow give Lambda43 and Lambda34. There is no free
covariance or multiplier left.

Conversely impose (22), eps=0,R>0 and define (23), allowing real
coefficients temporarily. The node0,1,2,4 flows and moments hold by
construction. For node3 the flow follows by summing the other flows.
Its moment can be checked explicitly: substituting (23) gives

 -w*Lambda32+v*Lambda34
   =-2*w*Bphys-w*r13-c*(w+v)*Lambda42+2*s*(C-v)
   =-2*(w+N)*Bphys+2*s*(C-v)=2*v*(1+Bphys),

where c(w+v)Lambda42=2*N*Bphys-w*r13 and
w+N=C-v=1+Bphys. Thus the full flow and Me=0 hold.

The original first row vanishes because aA=1; also M14=M24=0,
M13=-rho*Tstar. Direct substitution of Lambda12 gives

    M11=-D/beta^2+2*b*w*rho*Tstar/beta=rho^2*Tstar.

For the constant term in this identity use A^2=2A+1:
1-b^2+2A-2*A*b*Hpre/beta=-D/beta^2.
For the second equality use eps=0 and (8), which give
D=(2*b*w*x-x^2)*Tstar. The kernel then determines M12.
Finally (4), with all its costs zero and E row zero, determines
Tcut=Tstar: its positive Tcut coefficient is
ell*mu^2+a^2*b^2*mu^3, and (8) gives exactly that same coefficient
after subtracting the known J terms from R=rcoef*Tstar.
Me=0 now fixes every remaining entry except M44=nstar and proves
(24). The vectors vcol and dcol are independent, so full PSD is
equivalent to nstar>=0. This proves sufficiency as well as necessity.

The matched quadratic calibration has no extra freely selectable term:

    Q=jcoef^2*Tstar+dphys^2*nstar.                              (26)

Indeed pquad^T vcol=-jcoef and pquad^T dcol=dphys, by (8). Equation(13)
applies to the flow-correct array, so (24) proves (26). Consequently
nstar>=0 is equivalently Q>=jcoef^2*R/rcoef. At Q=0 this is
impossible when R>0; the WHOLE zero quadratic column is retained.

No word at C>=193/25 is exhibited satisfying eps=0 and (22)--(25).
Nor is it proved here that their entire high-capacity region is empty.

## 7. Zero boundaries and the unresolved complete comparison

At v=0 with ell>0, z=theta=qcorr=0, k=k0=kold=b^2/ell. Identity
(10) still holds and its Gram has sigma>0, but some displayed costs
vanish. Strict-cost conclusions, Pcost, jcoef divisions, (17)--(26),
and the positive-gap equality classification above are NOT used there.
One retains the undivided identities (1)--(7),(10),(13),(15).

At ell=0 in this domain one necessarily has v=0,bc=1. Then pquad=Avec,
R=mu*Q, and the old undivided gap is b*beta*Q. No division by ell,
v,jcoef,c1,c03 or c23 is made on this boundary. No formal gradient
is deleted at v=0. At R=0 or Q=0, use the undivided norm identities;
zero M-energy always kills its WHOLE original column.

The complete older step system includes the compact-domain cuts,
full quadratic and return tests, all clipping branches, all adjacent
and lifted triangles, C82/C84, the entire C83/C86 curvature families,
the later lifted return, the2948 first-row test and its stated
hypotheses, and the previously recorded scoped exclusion regions.
Shared19f1d27c0eb8a588 and554ec17f7f590bf0 were reread to retain this
complete list. Their entries are awareness, not accepted proof inputs.
The projected-core prism d3abee1b17da52da fails retained C82/C83;
the earlier scalar-family survivor b1592acfdb474277 /480b207356f9095f
fails the later partial-tail return. Neither is a survivor here.

Direct attempt one, completed algebraically, matches the ORIGINAL
rows at sharp equality and yields the explicit array (23). Direct
attempt two prices departures from it by (12)--(21), including the
actual pquad column, terminal row and first-row budget simultaneously.
Neither attempt establishes a point surviving ALL older conditions
and failing a new inequality, or a high-capacity feasible array.
Counterexample screening of those shortcuts is supplied by the two
shared rejected survivor families just described and the actual
covariance nonuniqueness example111139417631a944. No new low-capacity
calibration or unsupported survivor is manufactured.

The exact remaining STEP comparison is now the conjunction, for the
SAME h, of eps>=0, Q>=0, Qplus>=0, all inequalities(14),(16), and
R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)), against the complete older system. Its positive
region must then satisfy the original twenty coefficient signs and
the remaining full Gram entries. Equality is fully matched by(23),
but the open eps>0 region still has those simultaneous coordinates.
No independence or redundancy with the ENTIRE older system is proved.

The concrete simultaneous obstruction is therefore NOT a free choice
of terminal covariance: the actual 02 coefficient sign removes the
old capped equality optimizer, and the actual14/24 rows and pquad column
force (18),(26). Independent scalar-energy minimizers need not obey
these original-row equations. A further algebraic parameterization
without either a true old-system survivor or full high-capacity
matching would not close the assigned interface.

Fact search found no accepted theorem deciding this interface. Matlas
returned unrelated PL, exact-line-search, and minimax statements. Web
fallback recalled composition literature already audited in earlier
rounds. No external theorem is used and no new literature branch is
being rejected. The only accepted mathematical predecessor is
04219554e41d9750. No fact_submit is authorized or called; this entire
document remains one unverified proof attempt. The conditional seed
lift stays conditional, and the original exact-profile target is open.
