# A paid transverse carry and a performance ceiling for the entire retained history

Status: COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, final allocated
analytical round35. No numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay,
installation, subagent, external consult, or formal-verifier call is used.
Nothing here is a new fact. The original arbitrary-word asymptotic
exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

The new constructive object is a positive-parallel, genuinely transverse
cell, followed by a later reversal. One static two-dimensional function
retains every old query, pays its actual transverse minimizer displacement,
and returns its full outgoing affine-history/sublevel-distance state.
This is a nonempty two-transition rule on the explicitly stated family.
It is not an all-depth invariant. Its whole parameter family loses to an
accepted alternating-ray witness on the SAME word.

The separate obstruction is stronger than an impossible reversal: on a
specified family of supplied words, EVERY smooth convex extension of the
displayed old jets, in every dimension, with any changed minimum and any
future gradients, has a normalized payoff below the same-word accepted
reference. Thus merely changing the final chronology cannot make this
particular old history competitive there. The adversary may instead choose
its prefix using the whole word; that freedom is not excluded.

Accepted dependencies are fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth
interpolation inequality, factb98bb807d514e80d for K_n, fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a
for the alternating-ray witness, and fact0be8b54e0a33381c for B_n.
Every other ingredient used is proved inside this draft. Earlier max34
and retained-state findings are context, not premises.

## 1. Normalization, actual jets, and their forced return

Work at smoothness1 in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. Let e be a
unit vector, and retain the following three actual queried jets, with a
common additive value shift normalized to zero:

    x_A=3e,  g_A=e,   f_A=5/2;
    x_B=2e,  g_B=e,   f_B=3/2;
    x_C=-4e, g_C=-e,  f_C=7/2.                         (1.1)

They form the prefix with steps(1,6), starting at3e. They are legal:
the envelope of J_old(z)=|<e,z>| has scalar value t^2/2 for |t|<=1
and |t|-1/2 for |t|>=1. Its derivative on this line is clip(t,-1,1)e.
Its minimum is0 and its minimizer set is the hyperplane <e,z>=0.
The old proximal data z_i=x_i-g_i and H_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 are

    (z_A,H_A)=(2e,2), (z_B,H_B)=(e,1),
    (z_C,H_C)=(-3e,3).                                (1.2)

All three heights are strictly positive.

Let F be ANY convex differentiable1-smooth function retaining(1.1),
in any larger finite dimension, with a nonempty minimizer set. The signed
interpolation residual from accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.     (1.3)

For 2<=t<=3, convexity between A and B gives F(te)<=t-1/2.
The supporting inequality at B gives the reverse bound. Thus F(te)=t-1/2.
Applying Q_(B,t)<=0 gives ||grad F(te)-e||^2/2<=0, so the FULL gradient
is e, with no transverse component.

The next supplied step13/2 takes C to X=(5/2)e. Any following ordinary
list of nonnegative steps with sum S in[0,1/2] stays on [2e,(5/2)e].
Induction over its partial sums therefore forces every gradient in this
list, including the endpoint gradient, to be e. These conclusions use
actual old jets and smooth convexity; no new plane choice is assumed.

For a parameter delta in[0,1/2], with S+delta<1/2, prescribe

    b=3/2-S-delta >1,     Y=1+delta.

This checkpoint takes the endpoint of that ordinary list to Ye. The
complete word considered in Sections2--4 is

    h=(1,6,13/2, block_S, b, tail),                    (1.4)

where block_S has sum S, and tail is any finite nonnegative list. Write
T=sum(tail). No supplied step is changed to obtain a continuation. The
boundary S+delta=1/2, b=1 also obeys the proofs, but the stated b>1 region
is the interface relevant to the failed old next-block condition. In fact
b<=3/2<T+2 throughout this region, so the former next-mass condition
b>=T+2 fails for every member, including T=0.

## 2. Every changed minimum pays a radius cost

Let mu=min F in the value normalization(1.1), and let x_star be ANY
minimizer. Put t_star=<e,x_star>. In(1.3), the rows from B and C to
the minimizer, and the reverse row from the minimizer to B, give

    Q_(B,star)=t_star-mu <=0,
    Q_(C,star)=-t_star-mu <=0,
    Q_(star,B)=mu-1 <=0.

Consequently

    0<=mu<=1,       |t_star|<=mu.                     (2.1)

In particular, for the TRUE initial distance

    R=dist(3e,argmin F),       R>=3-mu>=2.             (2.2)

These constraints hold for every minimizer, including minimizers outside
the old geometric span. Extra dimensions cannot reduce the e-coordinate
part of this distance. Conversely, no old point is required to remain a
minimizer: (2.1)--(2.2) already permit a completely changed minimum and
minimizer set. This is the normalization used below.

Smoothness at the retained point B gives the value bound

    F(Ye) <= 3/2+(Y-2)+(Y-2)^2/2
           =1+delta^2/2=:C_delta.                     (2.3)

For completeness, the smoothness inequality follows by integrating the
gradient on the segment from B to Ye and using its Lipschitz constant1:
the remainder is at most the integral of t||Ye-B||^2 over0<=t<=1.
No assumption on the gradient at Ye enters(2.3).

## 3. Arbitrary subsequent gradients, with an explicit large-step payment

For any current point x, current gradient g, and h>=0, smoothness gives

    F(x-hg) <= F(x)-h(1-h/2)||g||^2.                  (3.1)

Also F(x)-mu>=||g||^2/2: apply the same inequality with h=1 and use
mu<=F(x-g). Thus each later step has the gap bound

    F(x-hg)-mu <= a(h)[F(x)-mu],
    a(h)=1 if 0<=h<=2, and a(h)=(h-1)^2 if h>2.       (3.2)

Let A_tail be the product of a(h) over the ACTUAL supplied tail, with
empty product1. Iterating(3.2) along its actual GD run gives

    2(F(x_end)-mu)/R^2
       <= A_tail * 2(C_delta-mu)/(3-mu)^2.            (3.3)

The derivative of the last rational factor with respect to mu is

    2(2C_delta-3-mu)/(3-mu)^3 <=0,

because delta<=1/2 and0<=mu<=1. Therefore EVERY such extension satisfies

    V_F:=2(F(x_end)-mu)/R^2
       <= A_tail*(2+delta^2)/9.                      (3.4)

If every tail step lies in[0,2], A_tail=1. There is no constant-gradient,
opposite-gradient, plane-count, fixed-rank, fixed-minimum, or fixed-value
assumption on the new samples. All future values and gradients may change
as allowed by one static F. The dependence on large future steps is the
displayed product, not a suppressed depth-dependent loss.

In particular, a proposed extension that aims at a score M>0 must have

    A_tail >= 9M/(2+delta^2).                         (3.5)

This necessary condition is not sufficient. It does not exclude a
different prefix chosen using the whole word, nor claim that a large-step
suffix must improve the score.

## 4. A uniform same-word performance obstruction

Suppose now that T<=3 and every tail step lies in[0,2]. The step6 at
index1 has preceding sum1 and subsequent sum8-delta+T in(1.4).
Its accepted single-cut score, from factb98bb807d514e80d, is

    K_cut=25/[4(17-2delta+2T)] <= K_n(h).             (4.1)

Combining(3.4) with(4.1),

    V_F/K_cut <=4(17-2delta+2T)(2+delta^2)/225
              <=22/25.                              (4.2)

Here is an exact check of the second inequality. Increasing T to3 leaves
the polynomial (23-2delta)(2+delta^2). Its second derivative is
46-12delta>0 on[0,1/2], so its maximum is at an endpoint. Its endpoint
values are46 and99/2. Multiplication by4/225 gives22/25. Thus the entire
retained history loses at least a factor22/25 to an accepted witness on
the SAME word, even after all possible changes in the future function.

There is a much stronger accepted reference here. In fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a,
select the checkpoints6 and13/2 in exactly this word. Its ordinary masses
are

    s_0=1, s_1=0, s_2=S+b+T=3/2-delta+T.

The accepted recursion gives

    m_0=1/2, O_0=0, m_1=5/2, O_1=1/2,
    z_term=53/4, m_old=1/2, e_term=1/4.

Every required positivity condition holds, and there is no same-ray
magnitude condition before the terminal stage. Since1<=s_2<=9/2,
z_term>=(1+s_2)m_old. Moreover

    q_opt=(53/4)/(4-2delta+2T) >=53/40>1/2,

and q_opt<=z_term/(1+s_2), because2s_2+1>=s_2+1. The actual accepted
alternating-ray construction therefore has payoff

    M_ray=1/2+(2809/16)/(4-2delta+2T)
           >=2889/160.                              (4.3)

This is the payoff of this particular accepted checkpoint selection,
not a claim that the entire F_ray_n(h) maximum was computed. It gives
M_ray<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h). Since(3.4) also gives V_F<=1/4,

    V_F/M_ray <=40/2889.                             (4.4)

This comparison is possible because the competing legal adversary
chooses different early jets on the SAME word. That is precisely the
freedom a whole-word adversary has. Equations(4.2)--(4.4) do not upper-bound
W_n(h), and they are not a minimax disproof or an obstruction to the
original affirmative theorem. These words are already handled strongly
by the accepted scalar construction.

For the previous actual six-step word

    h_A=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5,1/20),

delta=1/10 and T=1/20. The full-history bound and references become

    V_F<=67/300,
    K_6(h_A)=125/338,
    M_ray(h_A)=14201/312.                             (4.5)

The complete K maximum is

    max(10/309,125/338,605/4992,40/237699)=125/338.

The indexed terms are the cuts at6,13/2,6/5. The remaining cuts vanish.
The maximum follows from125/338>1/3 and every other term<1/8. The ray
score follows from(4.3) with denominator39/10:
1/2+2809*10/(16*39)=14201/312. These are exact symbolic substitutions,
not numerical evaluations.

The logical obstruction is therefore explicit: a rule that commits to
the old jets(1.1), then promises to recover even the accepted same-word
reference on every member of(1.4) by adjusting subsequent planes,
gradients, or the minimum, is false. The conclusion is conditional on
that particular past commitment; it is not an obstruction to choosing
different old jets after seeing the whole supplied word.

## 5. A genuine transverse escape with one static function

Now construct, rather than forbid, a new chronology. Use R^2 coordinates
(t,s) in the orthonormal directions e,v. Define

    q=(1/2)e+(2/5)v,       r_q=||q||^2=41/100,
    P(z)=1/2+<q,z>=1/2+t/2+2s/5,
    J(z)=max(0,t,-t,P(z),-2P(z)),
    f(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                   (5.1)

This fixes both affine offsets, the old component q_0=e/2, the fresh
component2v/5, and the reversal scale beta=2 TOGETHER. The normals of
the two added planes are q and -2q. Neither cell is optimized separately.
The old positive and negative planes, as well as the zero plane, remain.

Here is a direct justification of the static envelope construction.
The defining minimum exists, since J>=0 and the quadratic is coercive,
and it is unique by strict convexity. If a plane of normal g is active
at z and x=z+g, its supporting inequality shows that z minimizes the
objective: substituting any w gives an excess at least||w-z||^2/2.
Hence f(x)=J(z)+||g||^2/2. For two proximal points z(x),z(y), summing
their subgradient inequalities gives

    ||g(x)-g(y)||^2 <= <g(x)-g(y),x-y>,
    g(x)=x-z(x).

The supporting inequality and evaluation at z(x) bound the value
remainder between0 and||x-y||^2/2, proving differentiability with
gradient g(x). The displayed inequality proves its Lipschitz constant
is at most1. Convexity follows by joint convexity of the proximal
objective. Thus f is an everywhere finite convex1-smooth function.

In proximal coordinates the original residual(1.3) is equivalent to

    H_j >= H_i+<g_i,z_j-z_i>.

Checking every retained or future plane at each proposed proximal point
therefore checks both directions of EVERY original pair. Checking only
adjacent gradients is not the argument being used.

The minimum of J, and hence f, is0. Since J>=|t| and J>=max(P,-2P),
equality requires t=0 and P=0. Its unique minimizer in this plane is

    x_star=-(5/4)v,
    R^2=||3e-x_star||^2=9+25/16=169/16.               (5.2)

The minimum and minimizer set of f equal those of J: both nonnegative
terms in its defining minimum can be zero only at x=z in argmin J.
In higher dimension the unused perpendicular directions are free, and
the nearest minimizer and distance(5.2) are unchanged.

The old origin is NOT a minimizer of this new function. The old minimum
value0 is retained, but its nearest point moves by5/4 in a new direction.
No origin or radius is reset. A contract that also freezes the old
specific minimizer0 would exclude this construction; the legal-history
contract used here retains the queried jets and pays the true distance.

### Every inherited jet, including the remote blocking query, survives

At a positive old proximal coordinate(t,0) with t>=1,

    P(t,0)=(1+t)/2<=t,       -2P(t,0)<=0.

Thus all positive old and returned queries with proximal coordinates
2,1,3/2,13/10 retain their exact plane t. At(-3,0), P=-1 and-2P=2<=3,
so the negative plane also remains active. In particular no earlier
query-height constraint was omitted. The original physical positions
through the return are

    3e, 2e, -4e, (5/2)e, (23/10)e, (11/10)e,

using the exact steps1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5. All their old proximal heights
are positive. At the last point the newly selected gradient is q,
not a negative multiple of e.

### A whole supplied two-block region, with all endpoint comparisons

Append a nonnegative list of sum sigma in[0,1/10], then the prescribed
checkpoint6, then a nonnegative list of sum T in[0,1/20]. This is the
actual word family

    h_D=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5, block_sigma,6,block_T).      (5.3)

Its repaired chronology carries a positive e component after the small
checkpoint6/5, then uses the later supplied checkpoint6 for reversal.
Thus it is an escape from the failed reversal timing, not a claim that
the same reversal can be forced at6/5. That small checkpoint violates
the old next-mass cap even when the subsequent first block is empty.

The lists may have any finite length, may be empty when their sum is0,
and may contain zero steps. They are supplied in advance, and(5.1) is
the SAME single static function throughout every such run.

At a partial first-block mass u in[0,sigma], propose gradient q. Its
actual position and proximal point are

    x(u)=Ye-uq, z(u)=Ye-(u+1)q,       Y=11/10.

The selected height and comparisons with the old signed planes are

    P(z(u))=16/25-41u/100,
    P(z(u))-<e,z(u)>=1/25+9u/100>0,
    P(z(u))+<e,z(u)>=31/25-91u/100>0.                 (5.4)

For u<=1/10 the selected height is at least599/1000>0. Hence it also
dominates0 and-2P. These inequalities prove that q is the actual gradient
at every query in this entire first block and at its endpoint.

The checkpoint6 then gives the next start Ye-(sigma+6)q. At partial
second-block mass w in[0,T], propose gradient-2q. Put

    k=sigma+4-2w,    x(w)=Ye-(sigma+6-2w)q,
    z(w)=Ye-kq.

Then39/10<=k<=41/10, and

    <e,z(w)>=11/10-k/2<0,
    P(z(w))=21/20-(41/100)k<0,
    -2P(z(w))-|<e,z(w)>|=(8/25)k-1>=31/125>0.       (5.5)

The selected height is at least549/500>0. It dominates the opposite
old plane, P, and0 as well. This proves the second gradient and every
new/new or old/new plane comparison. The two added planes were checked
at ALL old queries before this calculation, so the future planes cannot
silently overwrite a retained value.

Since every comparison is affine in the partial sums, the same checks
cover all actual intermediate samples of arbitrary nonperiodic lists.
All old and new heights are strictly above the actual minimum0.

The terminal value and normalized payoff are exactly

    f_end=2[(sigma+5-2T)(41/100)-21/20],
    V_D(sigma,T)=16(100+41sigma-82T)/4225.             (5.6)

The perpendicular displacement through the first cell and its checkpoint
is -(2/5)(sigma+6)v. The paid initial transverse distance is5/4; its
squared contribution25/16 is already present in(5.2). These are different
quantities, neither of which has been discarded.

## 6. Exact actual word, full outgoing state, and its normalization

For sigma=1/10 and T=1/20 take one step in each ordinary list:

    h_D=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5,1/10,6,1/20).             (6.1)

The positions after Ye=(11/10,0) are

    (21/20,-1/25), (-39/20,-61/25), (-19/10,-12/5).

Their gradients are q,-2q,-2q respectively. The final proximal point is
p_end=(-9/10,-8/5), its height is59/50, and its gradient norm squared
is41/25. Thus

    f_end=59/50+41/50=2,       V_D=64/169.             (6.2)

Here is the complete outgoing retained state for this actual word.
Let u_out=q/||q||=(5e+4v)/sqrt(41), and G_out=2||q||=sqrt(41)/5.
Then x_end=p_end-G_out*u_out, exactly as required for the negative
outgoing boundary gradient. Write ell for its real slice coordinate.
The full retained envelope profile is

    Phi_out(ell)=max(
        |-9/10+5ell/sqrt(41)|,
        -59/100+sqrt(41)ell/10,
         59/50-sqrt(41)ell/5).                        (6.3)

Define the query profile, for each real a_test, by

    Psi_out(a_test)=min_(z,H in D)
                     [H-a_test*<u_out,z-p_end>],      (6.4)

where D contains ALL these actual proximal samples and heights:

    ((2,0),2), ((1,0),1), ((-3,0),3),
    ((3/2,0),3/2), ((13/10,0),13/10),
    ((3/5,-2/5),16/25),
    ((11/20,-11/25),599/1000),
    ((-19/20,-41/25),631/500),
    ((-9/10,-8/5),59/50).

No old query is deleted; coincident endpoints for zero steps can be
retained repeatedly without changing the minimum. For longer lists in
the same blocks, their intermediate contributions are affine between
the corresponding endpoint contributions, so endpoint compression within
each such block is an exact equality for Psi, not an assumption about
unrelated remote queries.

For the full sublevel-distance state, put

    P_ell=-59/100+sqrt(41)ell/10,
    A_ell=P_ell-1/2,
    t_free=(48+50A_ell)/41.

For r_level>=0 with -r_level/2<=P_ell<=r_level, set

    t_clip=min(r_level,max(-r_level,t_free)),
    Xi_out(ell,r_level)=(t_clip-3)^2
                         +(25/4)(A_ell-t_clip/2)^2.   (6.5)

For every other pair(ell,r_level), set Xi_out=+infinity. This is exactly
the squared distance from the ORIGINAL initial point(3,0) to the slice
of {J<=r_level} at <u_out,z-p_end>=ell.

Indeed the slice fixes q.z=A_ell and hence P(z)=P_ell. The sublevel
conditions are |t|<=r_level and -r_level/2<=P_ell<=r_level. Eliminating
s gives s=(5/2)(A_ell-t/2), so the distance squared is the quadratic
displayed in(6.5). Its derivative vanishes at t_free, and its positive
second derivative is41/8. Clipping therefore gives its exact constrained
minimum. At level0 the only feasible slice has

    ell=59/[10sqrt(41)], P_ell=0, A_ell=-1/2,

and(6.5) gives9+25/16=169/16. Thus the actual minimizer and normalization
are recovered from the retained sublevel state itself.

These formulas give a concrete nonempty local rule and exact output.
They do NOT assert that a subsequent arbitrary block will have the same
state shape or useful payoff. No repeated-depth loss estimate or all-word
invariant is implicit in them.

## 7. Compare both actual continuations to the accepted SAME-word references

On the actual eight-step word(6.1), the complete single-cut reference is

    K_8(h_D)=max(10/431,125/582,605/20608,
                40/2873997,125/1408)=125/582.          (7.1)

The indexed terms correspond to steps6,13/2,6/5,6. Each other cut has
zero numerator. The claimed maximum follows from125/582>1/5 and all
other terms<1/10. Our transverse witness exceeds this K score:

    64/169 > 125/582,

because64*582=37248>21125=125*169. This alone is NOT sufficient
performance progress: there is a stronger accepted reference available.

In fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a select the three checkpoints6,13/2,6 in(5.3).
Then s_0=1, s_1=0, s_2=7/5+sigma, s_3=T, and the accepted state is

    m_0=1/2, m_1=5/2, O_1=1/2,
    m_2=265/(48+20sigma)>=53/10,
    O_2=1/2, z_term=5m_2-1/2>=26,
    m_old=5/2, e_term=0.

The same-ray condition m_2>=m_0 holds. For T<=1/20,
z_term>=(1+T)m_old, and q_opt=z_term/(1+2T)>=260/11>5/2.
Thus every word in this whole two-block family has the accepted score

    M_ray_D=z_term^2/(1+2T) >=6760/11.                (7.2)

Equality holds for the exact word(6.1). Meanwhile(5.6) gives
V_D(sigma,T)<=8328/21125<2/5. Hence the ENTIRE explicit transverse
family satisfies

    V_D/M_ray_D <11/16900.                            (7.3)

No maximum over all checkpoint sets is asserted or needed. This one
accepted selection already strictly dominates the whole new family.

For an escape on the UNCHANGED six-step word h_A in(4.5), use the SAME
static function(5.1) and stop after the q-cell with mass1/20; do not
insert the later step6. Equations(5.4) prove every plane comparison.
The terminal position is(43/40,-1/50), its proximal point is
(23/40,-21/50), its height is1239/2000, and

    f_end=1649/2000,
    V_A_tilt=3298/21125.                              (7.4)

Every queried height remains positive and the true radius is still
sqrt(169)/4. This is a genuine nonreversing/transverse continuation of
the formerly trapped history on the ORIGINAL supplied word. It is better
than the old static Huber continuation11/90, since
3298*90=296820>232375=11*21125. Nevertheless

    3298/21125 <67/300 <125/338 <14201/312.             (7.5)

The first inequality is3298*300=989400<1415375=67*21125;
the second follows by cross multiplication, and the last is immediate
from14201/312>1 and125/338<1. Thus the actual escape, the full-history
upper bound, the single-cut score, and the stronger accepted ray score
are all ordered on the SAME word. The eight-step family is a separate
explicit family, not a repair obtained by silently changing h_A.

## 8. Comparison to the COMPLETE exact B_n benchmark

The accepted B_n recursion includes the split

    B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1)),
    G(1,D)=2+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8D)/2, B_0=1.

For D>=1, sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D+2, since the difference of the squares is
4(D-1)>=0. Induction gives B_n>=3n+1. This is a proved bound on the
ORIGINAL all-split recurrence; no numerical DP or dyadic substitute is
being used, and no exact closed form for B_6 or B_8 is asserted.

For U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, the actual witness comparisons are therefore

    V_A_tilt/U_6^ZJ=3298 B_6/21125
                       >=62662/21125>1,
    V_D/U_8^ZJ=64 B_8/169>=1600/169>1.                (8.1)

The accepted same-word ray witnesses are much stronger. These finite
benchmark exceedances do not imply anything adverse about a minimax
infimum over other supplied words. In particular the full-history
performance obstruction above is not a failure to reach U_n on this
family: these already easy words have accepted lower witnesses far
above U_n.

## 9. Zero steps, limiting cases, scaling, and exact scope

All ordinary blocks above can be split into arbitrary nonnegative lists.
Zero steps duplicate an actual jet; no proof divides by a step. Empty
blocks are included when their mass is0. The empty tail gives A_tail=1
and all bounds remain valid. In the explicit tilted family sigma=0 or
T=0 leaves strict positive heights by(5.4)--(5.5), even at the endpoints.
The boundary sigma=1/10,T=1/20 is attained, not merely a limit.

The bound(3.4) permits mu=0 and mu=1. At mu=1 some retained proximal
heights recentered by the minimum would be zero; allowing this boundary
only strengthens the obstruction. Strict-height extensions are included
as a smaller class. Delta=0, S=0, and S=1/2 are allowed whenever the
stated b condition is adjusted to b>=1 at its boundary. The uniform
performance bound extends continuously there. No positive gap or strict
height is claimed solely from a limiting zero-height construction.

Adding zero steps anywhere to either displayed actual word preserves
the actual trajectory, score, and accepted cut scores. It changes the
length n, and B_n>=3n+1 makes the benchmark comparisons still valid.
This creates arbitrarily long easy words, not a uniform result for all
words. The arbitrary-large-step suffix bound has its entire explicit
product A_tail; it is not a useful uniform-depth invariant by itself.

For any positive spatial scaling c, replace the objective by
f_c(x)=c^2 f(x/c), and start at3c e. Gradients and displacements scale
by c, heights and squared distances by c^2, and every normalized score
is unchanged. Translation and an additive value shift are harmless if
applied to every old and new datum together. Multiplying the objective
by L>0 and using physical steps h/L gives the corresponding L-smooth
statements. The zero spatial scale is excluded from normalized payoffs
because the initial point would then be a minimizer.

The new nonreversing cell is genuinely outside the earlier pure
reversal template. It does not contradict the earlier rigidity of the
first returned cell on[2e,3e], which is reproved in Section1: the tilt
begins only later at(11/10)e. Its next reversal occurs at a separately
supplied later checkpoint. No old gradient has been silently reset.

The all-extension obstruction fixes the old jets(1.1). It does not
require that every independently legal prefix be extendible by the
original adversary, and it does not refute any lookahead choice of a
different prefix. The full Phi/Psi/Xi state correctly retains the costs;
no insufficiency of that complete state is proved. The remaining
original-target interface is a whole-word choice of earlier history
and subsequent transitions with a useful uniform lower payoff on words
where the accepted baselines are small. Neither a new Bellman equation
nor the existence of the explicitly weak local rule supplies that step.

## 10. Retrieval, evidence status, and resource audit

TASK.md, own max34 closeout, targeted shared findings/dead ends, and the
latest guidance3e796d6ef28d3f87 were read. The current guidance expressly
preserves whole-word prefix choice and authorizes only the already

The full accepted alternating-ray statement and proof were read before

The mathematical skills used were query-memory, search-math-results,

Matlas returned2402.16232 Theorem3 on convex interpolation, redundant with

# Two retained modes with a long step and every tail sign

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft, max2 round30, the second/final of
the TWO analytical rounds allocated after completed baseline28 by TASK.md
and master_guidance4226374d0649bf55. No fact_submit is authorized or used.
Nothing new in this document is a verifier-accepted fact.

Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2, supplies the
ordered smooth-convex interpolation convention;04219554e41d9750,
Sections10--11, supplies the original primitive certificate identity and
the primitive composition identity. Every other ingredient is proved
jointly below. In particular the previous round29 draft is NOT a premise.

This draft closes the previously unclosed fast-tail-reversal regime on
the explicit word box below. There can be four fast reversals, both
modes can survive to terminal4, and the second physical step exceeds2.
All values, amplitudes, curvatures, original directed pairs and zero
branches are retained. The exact joint value minimum is computed, and
its negative part is precisely the negative part of an optimized pure
scalar family. A full original-space certificate with both tail steps
6/5 proves that the genuinely reversing regime itself contains a member
which survives EVERY valid old necessary test. That member is below the
required seed-capacity threshold.

The original goal and W=2R convention are unchanged. The result is an
energy-dominance statement for a restricted, explicitly stated family,
not a normalized objective comparison, a threshold seed, a global
no-seed theorem, or a resolution of the unrestricted exact-profile Goal.

Notation convention: every new local letter with an unsuffixed printed
name below denotes its version with suffix30. This includes a,b,c,d,
alpha,beta,u,v,p,z,ell,m,k,l,A_gap,B_gap,U,w,D,d_i^theta,B_theta,
J_theta,E_A,E_B,Delta,E_mix,j_min,rho,Conj,A_i,MinHull,R_true,t,delta,
kappa,C_right and T_mass. These do not redefine older project symbols.
The original h,s,Q_ij,V_h,Lambda_ij,L_flow,D_prim,M_prim keep their
accepted meanings. T_orig below is the original unit-diagonal prefix
matrix denoted T in accepted04219554e41d9750, not the unrelated project
single-cut scalar T. All further local notation is defined at first use.

## 1. The actual geometry and every sign branch

Fix

    h=(a,b,c,d),
    7/5<=a<=7/4,   2<b<=9/4,   0<=c,d<=a,                (1)
    1/b<=alpha<=1/a,   1/a<=beta<=1,   u,v>=0.             (2)

Put s=a+b+c+d and C=1+s. Initial coordinate reflections permit u,v>=0
without losing an amplitude sign. Both zero-amplitude endpoints remain.
For i=0,...,4 define

    p_i=product_(j<i)(1-alpha*h_j),
    z_i=product_(j<i)(1-beta*h_j),
    x_i=(u*p_i,v*z_i),
    g_i=(alpha*u*p_i,beta*v*z_i).                         (3)

The empty products equal1. These are the exact iterates and gradients of
one static convex 1-smooth base function

    F_base(x,y)=alpha*x^2/2+beta*y^2/2.

The recursion is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. No finite index is compressed.

Write

    ell=1-a*alpha, m=ell*(b*alpha-1),
    k=1-c*alpha, l=1-d*alpha.

Then ell,m>=0, k,l in[0,1], and

    p=(1,ell,-m,-m*k,-m*k*l),
    p_2<=p_3<=p_4<=p_1<=p_0=1.                           (4)

For the fast coordinate put r=a*beta-1 and q=b*beta-1. Both are
nonnegative, r<=3/4, and

    z=(1,-r,r*q,r*q*(1-c*beta),
                         r*q*(1-c*beta)*(1-d*beta)).      (5)

The function (a*beta-1)(b*beta-1) increases for beta>=1/a, because
its derivative is2ab*beta-a-b>=b-a>0. Consequently

    0<=z_2<= (a-1)(b-1)<=15/16<1.

Both tail multipliers lie in[-r,1]. Thus z_3,z_4<=z_2. If z_3<0,
then |z_3|<=r*z_2<=r. If z_4<0, precisely one of the two tail
multipliers is negative, so |z_4|<=r*z_2<=r. It follows that

    z_1<=z_3,z_4<=z_2<z_0=1.                             (6)

Only the order of nodes3 and4 varies:

    c*beta<=1:    z_1<=z_4<=z_3<=z_2<z_0,  branch A;
    c*beta>=1:    z_1<=z_3<=z_4<=z_2<z_0,  branch B.       (7)

Indeed z_4-z_3=-d*beta*z_3. The sign of d*beta-1 changes the
terminal sign but introduces no additional spatial-order branch.
At c*beta=1 both z_3 and z_4 vanish, and the two displayed orders
agree with ties.

For alpha strictly between its endpoints, u,v>0, and c,d<a, the slow
mode is retained with signs +,+,-,-,-. In branch B with c*beta>1
and d*beta>1, the fast signs are +,-,+,-,+ and the terminal is
nonzero. This open parameter set is nonempty, including at the
full-certificate word constructed in Section9. All endpoint and
product-zero cases in(1)--(7) remain part of the result.

## 2. Original primitive interface and the terminal-source price

For ALL distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, impose

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

The energy is exactly

    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)
          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_4||^2.     (8)

The full original certificate uses all twenty Lambda_ij>=0, with
zero diagonal, and

    e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2a,-2b,-2c,-2d,2s)^T,
    (T_orig)_ii=1, (T_orig)_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i,
    D_prim=diag(a(2-a),b(2-b),c(2-c),d(2-d),-s(s+2)).

Accepted04219554e41d9750 proves the exact implication and identity

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T_orig+T_orig^T*L_flow^T)/2,
    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.   (9)

A full seed requires M_prim PSD on ALL of R^5. Neither the two-mode
test geometry nor an optimal price flow replaces that requirement.

Put U_theta=theta(1-theta)/2 for theta=alpha,beta. At the base values,
direct substitution gives symmetric nonnegative slacks

    w_ij=-Q_ij(F_base)
        =u^2 U_alpha(p_i-p_j)^2+v^2 U_beta(z_i-z_j)^2.    (10)

For f_i=F_base(x_i)+epsilon_i the twenty inequalities are exactly
epsilon_i-epsilon_j<=w_ij. Let D_i be the shortest w-cost from the
ORIGINAL terminal source4 to i, with D_4=0. Nonnegative weights allow
cycles to be removed, so an attaining simple path exists even with
zero-cost edges. Both directed inequalities on each edge are present.
Following a path from4 to i gives epsilon_i-epsilon_4>=-D_i.
Since h_i>=0, the simultaneous all-value minimum therefore is

    V_min=u^2 B_alpha+v^2 B_beta
                                  -2sum_(i<4)h_i D_i,   (11)

where B_theta is the base scalar energy defined in(15) below. It is
attained at epsilon_i=-D_i: D_j<=D_i+w_ij proves every required
epsilon_i-epsilon_j<=w_ij.

For the dual orientation, route2h_i units from4 to each i<4 along
an attaining path. This has L_flow^T e=omega and cost2sum h_i D_i.
Conversely w_ij>=D_j-D_i implies that any nonnegative flow with this
same divergence costs at least2sum h_i D_i. This establishes the
exact terminal-source price. It does not assert PSD of that auxiliary
flow's original M_prim.

Section8 proves that the minimizing values come from an actual static
smooth convex function with a nonempty minimizer set. Thus no finite
value solution used here is an unattained relaxation.

## 3. Exact full joint distances: six routes, with all ties retained

The complete answer on the WHOLE parameter domain is

    D_4=0,
    D_3=w_43,
    D_1=min(w_41,w_43+w_31),
    D_2=min(w_42,w_43+w_32),                              (12)
    D_0=min(w_40,
            w_43+w_30,
            w_41+w_10,
            w_43+w_31+w_10,
            w_42+w_20,
            w_43+w_32+w_20).

Here is a proof covering every simple path, not merely selected ones.
For any real coordinate values t_i and a nonnegative weight U,

    U[(t_i-t_k)^2+(t_k-t_j)^2-(t_i-t_j)^2]
                              =2U(t_k-t_i)(t_k-t_j).     (13)

If node k lies outside the coordinate interval between i,j in BOTH
coordinates, removing an internal visit to k cannot increase the cost.
Node0 is a coordinatewise maximum by(4),(6), so it can be removed from
every path between core nodes{1,2,3,4}. Within the core, node1 is a
slow maximum and fast minimum; node2 is a slow minimum and fast maximum.
Either can therefore be removed whenever it occurs internally in a
core path. Hence the only routes to1 are4->1 or4->3->1 after such
shortcuts; the only routes to2 are4->2 or4->3->2; and the direct
route4->3 is optimal. This proves the first four lines of(12).
For a shortest path ending at0, its last core node is one of1,2,3,4.
Replace its prefix by an optimal path in the core. These possibilities
are exactly the six last-line candidates. All shortcuts are weak
inequalities, so ties, coincident points, and zero coordinate weights
cause no exception. Every listed route retains original node labels.

For fixed alpha,beta there is an exact finite amplitude elimination.
Normalize u^2+v^2=1 and set rho=u^2 in[0,1]. Every route cost in(12)
is affine in rho. Equation(11) is a continuous convex piecewise affine
function: it is an affine function minus nonnegative multiples of
the minima in(12). Its minimum on[0,1] is attained at an endpoint or
at an intersection of two distinct affine route costs from the SAME
minimum. There are at most15 such pairs for D_0, one for D_1 and one
for D_2, hence at most19 candidate locations including0 and1. Retain
only intersections in[0,1] with nonzero slope difference; evaluate the
FULL expression(11) at each. Parallel/identical lines cause no gap:
a flat minimizing interval has a boundary breakpoint or endpoint.
Inactive intersections merely give harmless extra candidate locations.

## 4. All-value scalar energies and both curvature branches

For real sampled coordinates, the shortest squared-distance path
between two samples uses every distinct intermediate sampled value in
monotone order. To prove this, list the nonnegative gaps between
consecutive distinct sorted values. The square of an edge spanning
several gaps is at least the sum of their squares. Every path between
the endpoints crosses each intervening gap at least once. Summing gives
the lower bound, attained by the monotone path. Ties add zero gaps.

The unweighted slow squared-distance minima from terminal4 are

    d_0^alpha=(1-p_1)^2+(p_1-p_4)^2,
    d_1^alpha=(p_1-p_4)^2,
    d_2^alpha=(p_2-p_3)^2+(p_3-p_4)^2,
    d_3^alpha=(p_3-p_4)^2,   d_4^alpha=0.                (14)

For any curvature theta, writing q_i=product_(j<i)(1-theta*h_j),
direct substitution into(8) gives the unit-amplitude base energy

    B_theta=theta sum_(i<4)h_i[1+theta(1-h_i)]q_i^2
                         -s*theta[1+C*theta]q_4^2.      (15)

The exact scalar value minimum is

    J_theta=B_theta-theta(1-theta)sum_(i<4)h_i d_i^theta. (16)

For the fast coordinate the COMPLETE formulas are:

    branch A (c*beta<=1):
    d_0^beta=(1-z_2)^2+(z_2-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,
    d_1^beta=(z_1-z_4)^2,
    d_2^beta=(z_2-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,
    d_3^beta=(z_3-z_4)^2;

    branch B (c*beta>=1):
    d_0^beta=(1-z_2)^2+(z_2-z_4)^2,
    d_1^beta=(z_1-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,
    d_2^beta=(z_2-z_4)^2,
    d_3^beta=(z_3-z_4)^2.                               (17)

In both branches d_4^beta=0. They agree at c*beta=1. No branch is
discarded when d*beta passes through1. Equations(11),(14)--(17),
applied in one dimension, and the construction in Section8 prove
actual attainment for all scalar minima J_theta. These are ALL-value
scalar tests, not merely the energies of the original quadratic values.

For u,v>0, alpha<1, beta<1, m>0, k>0, d>0, and c*beta>1,
there is a genuine strictly positive joint price. For example, scalar
optimal routes to1 are direct for the slow coordinate and via3 for
the fast coordinate. The extra cost of the two respective joint routes
is either

    2v^2 U_beta(z_4-z_3)(z_3-z_1),
    or 2u^2 U_alpha(p_4-p_3)(p_1-p_3),

both strictly positive under these strict conditions. Formula(12)
proves their minimum is the excess for D_1. Thus the coming dominance
proof pays an actual joint price; it does not assume scalar additivity.

## 5. A complete positive slow-coordinate budget

We prove, jointly here, that using scalar-fast-optimal routes for every
sink leaves at least alpha^2/40 of slow energy. The calculation has
two parts: a slow budget independent of fast signs, then the cost of
the sole fast-order swap.

First take these routes, denoted route system A:

    sink0:4->3->2->0; sink1:4->1;
    sink2:4->3->2;    sink3:4->3.                         (18)

They are fast-optimal throughout branch A by(7). Let E_A denote the
slow scalar base energy minus its price on exactly these routes. We
will prove

    E_A/alpha^2 >=487/3200 if a<=3/2,
    E_A/alpha^2 >=41/576   if a>=3/2.                    (19)

These designated-route energies are algebraic lower bounds, not
asserted feasible scalar value assignments. A designated route can
cost more than a scalar shortest path. This only strengthens the
required estimate: proving that its remaining energy is positive
also proves positivity after the smaller true scalar price.

Use theta=alpha. Start with the first two steps(a,b), whose terminal
point is -m, and append zero, one or two monotone slow tail steps
between0 and a. Write T_tail for their total, S=a+b+T_tail for the
whole current sum, and -y for the current terminal point. For a tail
step indexed j, write T_j for the tail sum up to and including that
step and q_j for its scalar point before the update. The corresponding
route system always sends sink0 through all tail nodes in reverse
chronology to2 then0, sends sink1 directly, and uses monotone tail
paths for tail sinks. Its energy is

    E_tail=1-(1+S*theta)^2 y^2
      -2theta(1-theta)[a(1+m)+b*ell*(ell+y)
               +theta sum_(tail j)(a+T_j)h_j q_j^2].     (20)

Here is the full telescoping derivation. For arbitrary scalar sampled
values put Acal_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2-f_i. Substituting this definition
in(8) and using x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i gives the exact identity

    V=x_0^2-(x_end+S*g_end)^2
                              -2sum h_i(Acal_i-Acal_end).

For values priced along the designated routes,
Acal_i=U_theta(q_i^2+d_i), where d_i is that route's unweighted
squared cost, and Acal_end=U_theta*y^2. On the negative monotone tail,

    q_i^2-y^2+d_i=2theta sum_(j>=i in tail)h_j q_j^2.

This follows term by term from
q_j^2-q_(j+1)^2+(q_j-q_(j+1))^2=2theta h_j q_j^2.
For sink0 the expression is2(1+m) plus the expression for sink2;
for sink1 it is2ell(ell+y). Collecting the coefficient of each tail
step yields a+T_j and proves(20).

Append a tail step t in[0,a] to an old terminal -y and old sum S.
The new magnitude is y(1-t*theta). Subtracting(20) at the two lengths
gives exactly

    E_new-E_old
      =theta^2*t*y^2{theta(S+t)[2(S+1)-theta*t*(S+t)]
                                      +2b(1-theta)}
        +2b*ell*y*theta^2*t*(1-theta).                   (21)

For an explicit coefficient check, the terminal factor changes by
(1+(S+t)theta)(1-t theta)=1+S theta-t(S+t)theta^2;
the new sum term in(20) is theta(S-b+t)t*y^2; and the term
b*ell*(ell+y) changes by -b*ell*y*t*theta. These three substitutions
give(21). All displayed terms are nonnegative: theta*t<=1 and

    2(S+1)-theta*t*(S+t)>=S+2-t>0

because S>=a+b and t<=a. Thus E_A is at least the energy E_empty
for the two-step word(a,b) with both sinks priced directly from2.

For that two-step word, set S_0=a+b. Direct expansion gives

    E_empty/theta^2
      =a(2-a)-b(b-2)ell^2
       -[2(1-theta)/theta^2]m
       -[2(1-theta)/theta^2+2S_0/theta+S_0^2]m^2.         (22)

One way to check(22) without expanding a long polynomial is to use
the two direct terminal interpolation lower bounds for f_0,f_1 in(8).
Their substitution gives

    theta^2[a(2-a)-b(b-2)ell^2]
     -2theta*m[(1-theta)(a+b*ell)+S_0*m]
     -S_0^2 theta^2 m^2.

The recursion gives theta(a+b*ell)=1+m. Dividing by theta^2 produces
exactly(22).

All following bounds follow from(1)--(2), with no older necessary
condition imported:

    (1-theta)/theta^2<=b(b-1)<=45/16,
    S_0<=4,
    2(1-theta)/theta^2+2S_0/theta+S_0^2<=317/8<40,
    ell<=1-a/b,
    m<=(b-a)^2/(4ab).                                   (23)

The first uses the decreasing function (1-theta)/theta^2 on(0,1].
The last follows by completing the square in
(1-a theta)(b theta-1); its bound increases with b/a>=1.

For7/5<=a<=3/2, one has a(2-a)>=3/4, ell<=17/45<2/5,
m<=289/5040<1/16, and b(b-2)<=9/16. Thus(22) is at least

    3/4-9/100-45/128-5/32=487/3200.

For3/2<=a<=7/4, one has a(2-a)>=7/16, ell<=1/3 and m<=1/24.
The lower bound becomes

    7/16-1/16-15/64-5/72=41/576.

Together with(21), these estimates prove(19), including m=0, ell=0,
zero tail steps and vanished terminal products.

Now consider branch B, c*beta>=1. Fast-optimal routes are instead

    sink0:4->2->0; sink1:4->3->1;
    sink2:4->2;    sink3:4->3.                           (24)

Denote their slow energy by E_B. Define the nonnegative slow gaps

    A_gap=p_3-p_2=m*c*alpha,
    B_gap=p_4-p_3=m*k*d*alpha.

Replacing(18) by(24) increases the slow price by exactly

    Delta=4U_alpha B_gap[(a+c)A_gap+b(ell+m*k)],
    E_B=E_A-Delta.                                      (25)

For sinks0 and2, replacing the two gaps by their direct edge adds
2A_gap*B_gap to each unweighted route cost. For sink1 the difference
B_gap^2+(p_1-p_3)^2-(p_1-p_4)^2 is2B_gap(p_1-p_3).
Multiplication by the original sink demands2a,2b,2c proves(25).

In branch B, c>=1/beta>=1. Therefore

    0<=k<=1-alpha<=5/9,   b*k<=5/4,   c*alpha<=1.

Equation(25) yields

    Delta/alpha^2
      =2(1-alpha)m*k*d[b*ell+m((a+c)c*alpha+b*k)].        (26)

If a<=3/2, the bracket is at most

    9/10+(3+5/4)/16=373/320<4/3,

and its prefactor is at most

    2(5/9)(1/16)(5/9)(3/2)=25/432<1/16.

Hence Delta/alpha^2<1/12. Since487/3200>1/8, (19) gives
E_B/alpha^2>1/24>1/40 in this case.

If a>=3/2, the bracket is at most

    3/4+(7/2+5/4)/24=91/96<1,

and the prefactor is at most175/3888. Consequently

    E_B/alpha^2>=41/576-175/3888
                         =407/15552>1/40.               (27)

The last comparison is the integer inequality16280>15552. We have
proved the uniform budget, on BOTH branches and ALL boundaries,

    E_A>=alpha^2/40 in branch A,
    E_B>=alpha^2/40 in branch B.                         (28)

No interpolation optimum at the base origin, terminal annihilation,
scalar sufficient-certificate characterization, or hidden old test was
used in this budget.

## 6. Complete comparison, all amplitudes and both curvatures

For each sink, its shortest joint cost is no greater than the joint
cost of its designated route from(18) or(24). The fast component of
those routes attains every scalar fast minimum in(17). By(11),(28),

    V_min>=u^2 E_A+v^2 J_beta in branch A,
    V_min>=u^2 E_B+v^2 J_beta in branch B,
    V_min>=alpha^2*u^2/40+v^2 J_beta throughout(1)--(2).  (29)

Let E_mix(h) be the minimum of the EXACT expression(11)--(12) over
the closed curvature intervals(2) and u^2+v^2=1. It exists, since
these sets are compact and the expression is continuous. Define

    j_min(h)=min_(1/a<=beta<=1)J_beta(h).

Pure fast amplitude u=0,v=1 attains J_beta. Since alpha>=1/b,
(29) proves the complete negative-part comparison

    E_mix(h)>=min(1/(40b^2),j_min(h)),
    min(0,E_mix(h))=min(0,j_min(h)).                     (30)

If j_min<0, every mixture has energy at least j_min and the pure fast
minimizer attains it; if j_min>=0 every mixture is nonnegative. This
eliminates ALL slow curvatures and ALL amplitude ratios for the sign
question. It covers joint-value optima with genuine extra pricing.

Fast-curvature optimization is also specified exactly. On each of the
two closed subintervals separated by beta=1/c, if that point lies in
[1/a,1], equations(15)--(17) give an explicit polynomial of degree at
most10. If c=0 there is only branch A. The minimum j_min is the
minimum of the values at1/a,1, any included breakpoint1/c, and the
real roots of the corresponding polynomial derivative in each
subinterval. If a derivative vanishes identically, either endpoint
supplies its constant value. This includes collapsed intervals,
multiple roots, equality of branches, and every product-zero case.
No root evaluation or machine algebra is needed or used.

For precision about the COMPLETE old system, define S_scalar(h) as
the following channel: for EVERY theta in[0,1], the scalar samples
q_i=product_(j<i)(1-theta h_j), gradients theta*q_i, and EVERY joint
choice of values satisfying all original ordered interpolation rows
and realized by a smooth convex function with a minimizer must have
nonnegative V_h. Section8 proves that its minima are actual legal
functions. This is the FULL value-optimized scalar channel. The only
old necessary inequalities USED in this draft are

    J_beta(h)>=0 for every beta in[1/a,1],               (31)

which are contained in S_scalar. No old cut, return, triangle or
curvature condition is assumed in the proof of(29).

Let N_old(h) be the conjunction of every other old necessary condition,
with every original parameter range retained. Equation(30) gives

 [N_old(h) and S_scalar(h) and nonnegativity of this entire joint family]
                    iff [N_old(h) and S_scalar(h)]      (32)

throughout(1). Thus all the other conditions remain in the comparison;
none is silently dropped. The box(1) is a restriction of the studied
family, not a claimed necessity theorem for every four-step seed.

There remains a precise limitation: this draft does NOT prove that a
smaller list consisting only of unrepriced quadratics, finitely listed
cuts/returns and C83 is equivalent to S_scalar. No independence from
that smaller list is asserted. The comparison is the fully specified
scalar-witness dominance(29)--(32). It also does NOT compare the
different witnesses' terminal objective payoffs after their true
initial distances have been normalized.

## 7. Boundaries and formal-coordinate audit

The minima and estimates use weak orders throughout. If c=0 or d=0,
corresponding actual samples coincide and both directed inequalities
force their values to agree. These nodes remain in(9)--(12); no formal
gradient is deleted from the original PSD interface.

At alpha=1/b the slow product vanishes at2; at alpha=1/a it vanishes
at1. At beta=1/a the fast product vanishes at1. At beta=1/c or1/d,
when the reciprocal belongs to(2), it vanishes at that tail update.
Equations(4)--(30) include all these cases. The branch boundary
c*beta=1 is covered by either route system, with equal fast costs.
The boundary d*beta=1 changes no sorted-order formula.

If beta=1, U_beta=0 and the fast value slacks vanish; the proof still
uses legal routes with zero fast costs. If an amplitude vanishes,
(11) reduces to its exact scalar minimum. Equal curvatures can occur
only at alpha=beta=1/a, with all later products zero; the formulas
still agree. Zero costs, coincident nodes, parallel price lines, and
ties need no division by a sign or product. The face b=2 has the same
formulas by continuity, although the selected family has b>2.

## 8. Static attainment, actual minimizers, and true initial distance

Set f_i=F_base(x_i)-D_i with D_i given by(12), and on R^2 define

    cjet_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i,
    Conj_i(w)=cjet_i+<x_i,w-g_i>+||w-g_i||^2/2,
    Conj(w)=max_(0<=i<=4)Conj_i(w),
    F(x)=sup_w[<x,w>-Conj(w)].                           (33)

Every original finite node, including the terminal, occurs. The base
origin is NOT required to remain a minimizer.

For completeness, Conj is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex and
has quadratic growth, so the supremum in(33) has a unique maximizer
w(x). The two strong subgradient inequalities imply

    <x-x',w(x)-w(x')>>=||w(x)-w(x')||^2.

Thus w is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the defining maxima at x and x'
gives

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<w(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2,

which proves differentiability with grad F=w. The supremum
representation gives convexity and finiteness. Direct expansion gives
Conj_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij<=0, with equality for j=i. Therefore
Conj(g_i)=cjet_i and x_i is a subgradient of Conj at g_i. The unique
maximizer condition proves F(x_i)=f_i and grad F(x_i)=g_i. This is
one actual static smooth function realizing the whole original word.

Define

    A_i=Conj_i(0)=D_i-u^2 U_alpha p_i^2-v^2 U_beta z_i^2,
    A_max=max_i A_i, Active={i:A_i=A_max},
    y_i=x_i-g_i=((1-alpha)u*p_i,(1-beta)v*z_i).

Then

    MinHull=argmin F=conv{y_i:i in Active},
    min F=-A_max.                                       (34)

Indeed the directional derivative of Conj at0 is
max_(i in Active)<y_i,w>. Its subgradient set is exactly the displayed
convex hull: elements of the hull give supporting inequalities, and
strict separation rules out a point outside it. The condition grad
F(x)=0 is equivalent to x in that subgradient set, proving(34).

If u^2+v^2>0, every i satisfies p_i,z_i<=1, so

    <x_0,x_0-y_i>
      =u^2[1-(1-alpha)p_i]+v^2[1-(1-beta)z_i]
      >=alpha*u^2+beta*v^2>0.

Thus x_0 lies outside MinHull and R_true=dist(x_0,MinHull)>0.
An exact finite formula for R_true^2 is the minimum of distances to
all active vertices and all segments between two active vertices.
For distinct y_i,y_j the segment candidate is

    tau_ij=min(1,max(0,<x_0-y_i,y_j-y_i>/||y_j-y_i||^2)),
    ||x_0-y_i-tau_ij(y_j-y_i)||^2.                       (35)

Coincident vertices use their vertex candidate. A closest point to an
exterior point in a planar convex hull lies on a vertex or edge, and
every listed segment is in the hull; this proves the formula.

Since D_4=0, this actual attaining function has TRUE normalized payoff

    2(F(x_4)-min F)/R_true^2
                =2[F_base(x_4)+A_max]/R_true^2.          (36)

For a closest minimizer x_star, the function
F_scaled(x)=[F(x_star+R_true*x)-min F]/R_true^2 and the initial point
(x_0-x_star)/R_true give a 1-smooth instance at true distance1, with
the same word and payoff. Multiplying F_scaled by any L>0 restores the general smoothness
normalization. Formula(30) only compares primitive energies under
u^2+v^2=1; it makes no payoff comparison after these potentially
different true radii are paid.

The same construction with one coordinate proves the scalar attainment
claims in Sections4 and6, with a nonempty minimizer set and positive
initial distance whenever that coordinate's amplitude is nonzero.
Adding an actual minimizer as the optimum sample supplies every
optimum interpolation row; none is imposed at an incorrect base point.

## 9. A full-certificate survivor in the genuinely reversing regime

We give an actual original-R^5 certificate, not a sparse auxiliary
price flow. This jointly proved example establishes that the NEW
tail-reversal regime is not already excluded by the complete old
necessary system. It does not reach the threshold193/25.

Let

    a=sqrt(2), t=6/5, C_right=1+2t=17/5,
    delta>0 solve delta(22/5+a+delta)=(17/5)(1+a),
    h=(a,1+delta,t,t), T_mass=22/5+a+delta,
    kappa=(T_mass/C_right)^2.                            (37)

The left side of the delta equation is strictly increasing for delta>=0.
At delta=1 its difference from the right side is2-(12/5)a<0.
At delta=5/4 the difference is(293-172a)/80>0 because a<3/2.
Therefore1<delta<5/4, so(37) lies in(1). For every beta in(5/6,1)
and alpha strictly between1/(1+delta) and1/a, both modes are retained
and the fast mode has FOUR strict reversals. Its slow tail factors
are positive since t<a. The amplitude interior u,v>0 is nonempty.

Use these original multipliers, every other off-diagonal coefficient
being zero and every diagonal coefficient being zero:

    Lambda_01=2,              Lambda_10=2a+2,
    Lambda_14=delta(kappa-1),
    Lambda_21=t(kappa-1),     Lambda_23=2kappa,
    Lambda_24=t(kappa-1),
    Lambda_31=t(kappa-1),     Lambda_32=(2t+2)kappa,
    Lambda_34=4(t+1)kappa+t(kappa-1),
    Lambda_41=kappa-1,        Lambda_43=(8t+4)kappa.        (38)

Every coefficient is nonnegative. The exact full formal identity is

    V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij
      =kappa[(2-t^2)||g_2-g_3||^2
                         +4(1+t-t^2)||g_3-g_4||^2]
      =(kappa/25)[14||g_2-g_3||^2+76||g_3-g_4||^2].      (39)

Here are all child calculations and the join, so no unverified earlier
certificate is imported. For a one-step word a=sqrt(2), direct
substitution gives

    V_(a)+2Q_01+(2a+2)Q_10=0.                           (40)

For the two-step child(t,t), on local indices0,1,2, start with the
flow2t Q_10+4t Q_21. Its value coefficients cancel those of V, and
its gradient quadratic is

    t(2-t)||g_0||^2+t(4-t)||g_1||^2-4t^2||g_2||^2
       +2t(t-1)<g_0,g_1>+4t(t-1)<g_1,g_2>.

For either adjacent t-update i->j=i+1, direct expansion gives

    Q_ij+Q_ji=(1-t)||g_i||^2+||g_j||^2
                                            +(t-2)<g_i,g_j>.

Add2(Q_01+Q_10)+4(t+1)(Q_12+Q_21). The resulting diagonal
coefficients are2-t^2, 6+4t-5t^2, and4(1+t-t^2). Its cross
coefficients are-2(2-t^2) and-8(1+t-t^2), with no0,2 cross term.
It is exactly

    (2-t^2)||g_0-g_1||^2+4(1+t-t^2)||g_1-g_2||^2.

The child multipliers are consequently2,2t+2,4(t+1),8t+4 on
01,10,12,21 respectively. At t=6/5 the two squared coefficients
are14/25 and76/25, both positive.

The accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11 primitive join applies to
child capacities1+a and C_right, bridge excess delta and scale kappa
from(37). Its added interpolation combination is

    (kappa-1)[t Q_21+t Q_31+Q_41
                             +delta Q_14+t Q_24+t Q_34].

Embed(40) on original nodes0,1 and the proved right-child certificate
on2,3,4. The accepted exact join identity gives precisely(38)--(39).
For a separate flow check,

    (kappa-1)(C_right-delta)=2(1+a+delta)

follows from delta*T_mass=(1+a)C_right. Nodes0,2,3 have net flows
-2a,-2t,-2t. Node1 has net flow
2a+(kappa-1)(delta-C_right)=-2(1+delta), and node4 has net
flow4t+(kappa-1)(C_right-delta)=2(a+1+delta+2t)=2s.
Thus L_flow^T e equals exactly the original omega. The remainder(39)
is PSD on all FIVE independent formal gradients; no quotient,
rank restriction, selected gradient equality, or missing pair is used.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory on this word has V_h>=0 by(39)
and Q_ij<=0. Consequently EVERY valid old necessary condition, with
its whole original parameter domain, holds for(37); a negative actual
witness to any one of them would contradict(39). This proves
nonvacuity against the complete older system, including the genuinely
reversing open curvature domain, rather than only checking a subset.
The geometry can nevertheless have the positive joint price from
Section4, which is paid by(29).

Finally T_mass<22/5+3/2+5/4=143/20<193/25. This is explicitly
a below-threshold full certificate, not the missing threshold seed.
As with the rest of this document it remains an UNVERIFIED draft.

## 10. Structural conclusion and exact unclosed regimes

On(1)--(2), both retained signed modes and every fast tail sign have
now been handled in one joint draft. Adding tail reversals changes
the scalar fast ordering only by swapping3 and4. That swap can
create pricing conflicts at more than one sink, and the proof pays
their TOTAL cost(25), not merely a positive price at one chosen sink.
The retained slow mode has a uniformly positive two-step reserve;
monotone slow-tail extension preserves it, and the extra swap cost
uses less than that reserve. This is the precise reason for(30).

Do not repeat this closed family by changing beta, either tail sign,
values, amplitudes, product-zero boundaries or shortest paths.
Equations(11)--(32) already optimize or compare all of them.

The precise regimes not settled by this proof include:

* A slow curvature outside[1/b,1/a], so its early and/or tail signs
  differ and the specific positive reserve(19) need not apply.
* Word parameters outside(1), in particular tails exceeding a, or
  bounds allowing z_2>1 or z_3/z_4<z_1. The two extreme-node shortcut
  and the swap-cost estimate need to be reconsidered there.
* Two curvatures in other spectral intervals, more retained modes,
  and nonseparable legal static bases.
* A comparison that starts only from a smaller unrepriced quadratic /
  finite-cut / C83 list, without the complete scalar value channel.
* True-distance-normalized payoff dominance between mixed and scalar
  attaining functions; only primitive-energy dominance is proved.
* The full variable-word/twenty-multiplier/ambient-R^5 threshold seed
  question and the original unrestricted exact-profile Goal.

These are mathematical scope statements, not a new round allocation.

# C34. Exact coordinate-path projection relay and its near-critical obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier or numerical solver was used.
This is an exact obstruction for the defined coordinate-flow family, not an
upper bound on unrestricted W and not a primitive-capacity theorem.

## 1. Actual correlated real vectors and the whole trajectory

Partition a nonnegative schedule into N ordinary blocks of masses S_i,
separated by checkpoints b_i>1, 0<=i<N-1. Each ordinary block can be an
arbitrary ordered list with that mass. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and
T_i=S_i+b_i=M_i+L_i for i<N-1. Choose an artificial terminal parameter
T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1). Let e_0,...,e_(N-1) be the standard orthonormal basis.

Choose alpha_i>0, beta_i>=0 for i<N-1, with beta_(N-1)=0, and impose

    T_(i+1) alpha_(i+1)=T_i beta_i.

For a nonzero terminal payoff all beta_i must be positive. Define

    p_i=alpha_i e_i-beta_i e_(i+1)  (i<N-1),
    p_(N-1)=alpha_(N-1)e_(N-1),
    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),
    F(x)=max_{p in C} (<x,p>-||p||^2/2).

The strict concavity of the maximization gives its unique maximizer P_C(x).
The projection variational inequality proves
||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2<=<P_C(x)-P_C(z),x-z>, hence F is convex and
differentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient P_C. Differentiability follows by
bounding the two maximization differences between the corresponding linear
forms and then using the displayed Lipschitz estimate. F>=0 and F(0)=0.

Start at x_init=T_0 alpha_0 e_0. At phase i, after ordinary partial mass
u in [0,S_i], prescribe

    x_i(u)=(T_i-u)alpha_i e_i+u beta_i e_(i+1).

At the checkpoint, subtracting b_i p_i gives T_i beta_i e_(i+1), which is
the next prescribed start. Thus the SAME whole GD trajectory is realized
if P_C(x_i(u))=p_i at every actual partial mass.

Write y_i(u)=x_i(u)-p_i. Its two coordinates are
(T_i-u-1)alpha_i and (u+1)beta_i, both nonnegative. Put

    delta_i(u)=<y_i(u),p_i>
              =(T_i-u-1)alpha_i^2-(u+1)beta_i^2.

For j<=i-2 and j>=i+2, <y_i(u),p_j>=0. For j=i-1 this inner
product is -beta_(i-1)(T_i-u-1)alpha_i<=0. For j=i+1 it is
(u+1)beta_i alpha_(i+1). The comparison with zero is 0<=delta_i(u).

Consequently ALL normals against ALL vertices and zero hold if and only if

    L_i alpha_i^2-M_i beta_i^2
                    >= M_i beta_i alpha_(i+1),     i<N-1,       (1)

together with T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1). Necessity uses the actual last query in
each ordinary block, including an empty block's only query. Sufficiency
uses the fact that delta_i(u)-(u+1)beta_i alpha_(i+1) decreases with u.
The terminal phase has only its nonnegative own normal and nonpositive
comparison with its preceding vertex. Thus no remote condition is omitted.

Using the transfer relation, (1) is precisely

    (beta_i/alpha_i)^2
          <= L_i T_(i+1)/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))].                    (2)

The term M_i beta_i^2 in (1) records the mass already accumulated in the
next coordinate during the ordinary block. Replacing it by beta_i^2
would give a false, much stronger relay.

The correlations are explicit:
<p_i,p_(i+1)>=-beta_i alpha_(i+1)<0, all nonadjacent correlations zero.
The p_i are linearly independent because their coordinate matrix is
triangular with positive diagonal. Thus C is an actual N-simplex,
not a formal Gram matrix.

## 2. Actual normalization and exact optimal payoff

The minimizer set is C^polar={z:<p_i,z><=0 for all i}: these inequalities make every maximand nonpositive, while if <p_i,z>>0, a sufficiently small positive multiple of p_i in C gives a positive maximand. The terminal
inequality forces z_(N-1)<=0. Backward substitution then forces every
coordinate z_i<=0. Therefore 0 is a closest minimizer to x_init, whose
true squared distance to the minimizer set is T_0^2 alpha_0^2.

At the final point, with D=2S_(N-1)+1,

    2F_end / dist(x_init,argmin F)^2
      = (2T_(N-1)-D)/T_(N-1)^2
                         product_(i=0)^(N-2)(beta_i/alpha_i)^2. (3)

For fixed T_(N-1), equality in every bound (2) maximizes this expression.
It gives legal positive parameters recursively from any alpha_0>0. Thus
the exact optimum for this family is

    E(T)=(2T-D)/T^2
        product_(i=0)^(N-2) [L_i T_(i+1)/(M_i(T_i+T_(i+1)))].   (4)

For N>=2 let C_last=T_(N-2). All T dependence in (4), up to a positive
constant, is

    phi(T)=(2T-D)/[T(T+C_last)],  T>=M_(N-1).

Its derivative has numerator -2T^2+2DT+D C_last. Therefore its unique
maximum occurs at

    T_opt=(D+sqrt(D^2+2D C_last))/2 >= D >= M_(N-1),

and

    phi(T_opt)=2/[D+C_last+sqrt(D^2+2D C_last)].

For N=1, (3) is maximized at T=D and gives 1/D, the global Huber value.

The primitive defect is tracked on the same normalized trajectory. Let s be
the mass of the ENTIRE schedule and let
V=2 sum_t h_t(F_t-F_end)+sum_t h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
  -s(s+1)||g_end||^2.
At every projection query F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2, so
2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
=||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2. Telescoping gives

    V=||x_init||^2-||x_end+s g_end||^2,

and therefore, with T=T_(N-1),

    V/dist(x_init,argmin F)^2
      =1-[(T-S_(N-1)+s)/T]^2
                   product_(i<N-1)(beta_i/alpha_i)^2.           (5a)

Equations (3) and (5a) report BOTH the normalized objective and the normalized
primitive defect; neither is inferred from the sign of the other.

## 3. Every dyadic-depth version fails on the near-critical word

Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and
H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).
Let S_j be its mass and M_j=S_j+1. Direct geometric summation gives

    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2).

Here 0<A<1: c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1 while ac=1-1/m<1.
Also 2<c<49/20 and c-2<9/20. For example sqrt(2)<99/70 gives
lambda<169/70, and (169/70)(129/128)<49/20.

Choose any r in [0,m-1]. Partition H_m into N=2^k copies of H_r, where
k=m-r, separated by its actual higher-level checkpoints. Then all M_i=M=M_r
and

    L_i=a c^(r+nu_2(i+1)),  0<=i<N-1,
    q=a c^r/M <= c-2 < 9/20,
    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1))).

Here nu_2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer j.
In particular the first and last nonterminal T_i equal T_0=M(1+q).
Put D=2M-1. Exact counting of binary valuations gives

    product_(i<N-1)(L_i/M)=q^(N-1)c^(N-k-1)
                         =(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k).                   (5)

For internal transitions i<N-2, every checkpoint of level ell>=1
is flanked by two level0 checkpoints. Therefore

    product_(i<N-2)(1+T_(i+1)/T_i)
      = product_(ell=1)^(k-1)
          [(T_0+T_ell)^2/(T_0 T_ell)]^(2^(k-ell-1))
      >= 2^(N-2),                                           (6)

where T_ell=M(1+q c^ell). Each bracket is at least4 by
(T_0-T_ell)^2>=0. There are (N-2)/2 brackets, with multiplicity.

Telescoping the T_(i+1)/T_i factors in (4) and retaining the terminal
factor gives the exact formula

    E_opt = phi(T_opt) (qc)^(N-1)c^(-k) /
            product_(ell=1)^(k-1)
             [(T_0+T_ell)^2/(T_0 T_ell)]^(2^(k-ell-1)).        (7)

In particular phi(T)<= (2T-D)/T^2 <=1/D, so

    E_opt <= [2/(D c^k)] (qc/2)^(N-1).                       (8)

Let K=1/(2M_m-1). From the formula for M_j and q<=c-2,

    M_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k] <=M c^k.

Consequently for k>=2,

    E_opt/K <= 4 (qc/2)^(N-1)
             <4 (441/800)^(N-1)
             <=4 (441/800)^3 <1.                            (9)

Every genuinely multitransition dyadic-depth relay in this family is thus
strictly below K, uniformly in its free scalar parameters. If k tends to
infinity, its ratio to K tends to0 at least geometrically in 2^k.

For completeness k=1 is also below K. Set epsilon=1/M in (0,1]. The exact
two-phase optimum divided by K is

    2q[2(2+q)-epsilon] /
     [3+q-epsilon+sqrt((2-epsilon)(4+2q-epsilon))].

It is bounded above by

    R(q)=4q(2+q)/[2+q+sqrt(3+2q)].

This function increases for q>=0: write it as
4q/[1+sqrt(3+2q)/(2+q)]; the ratio in the denominator decreases,
since its derivative has numerator -(1+q). Therefore

    R(q)<R(9/20)=441/[245+10sqrt(390)]<1;

the last inequality follows from 196^2<39000.

Allowing r=m gives the single-phase Huber construction and attains K.
Thus the optimal payoff of the UNION of all these dyadic-depth incidence
relays is exactly K. This does not claim that all simplex walks, all
correlated walks, or all smooth-convex functions have payoff at most K.

## 4. Why the obstruction is useful and what it does not close

The family is legal in dimension N with every old/future normal retained,
uses N distinct mutually overlapping gradients, and has an exact
closest-minimizer calculation. Its failure is quantitative; no translated
negative-energy sign is used. The penalty in (6) comes from adjacent
coordinate transfer and survives the near-critical perturbation qc=1+O(1/m).

A tree transfer can avoid following N consecutive fresh coordinates, but it
must retain waiting coordinates. Every waiting future vertex then contributes
a positive normal comparison throughout the intervening phases. That is a
different construction class, not covered by (9).
